From Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann system to two-fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell system with Ohm’s law: convergence for classical solutions Thanks: August 7, 2026
Ning Jiang, Yi-Long Luo

Abstract
. For the two-species Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann (VMB) system with the scaling under which the moments of the fluctuations to the global Maxwellians formally converge to the two-fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell (NSFM) system with Ohm’s law, we prove the uniform estimates with respect to Knudsen number ε \varepsilon for the fluctuations. As consequences, the existence of the global in time classical solutions of VMB with all ε ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] \varepsilon\in(0,1] is established. Furthermore, the convergence of the fluctuations of the solutions of VMB to the classical solutions of NSFM with Ohm’s law is rigorously justified. This limit was justified in the recent breakthrough of Arsénio and Saint-Raymond [ 5 ] from renormalized solutions of VMB to dissipative solutions of incompressible viscous electro-magneto-hydrodynamics under the corresponding scaling. In this sense, our result gives a classical solution analogue of the corresponding limit in [ 5 ] . Keywords. two-species Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann system; two-fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell system; Ohm’s law; global classical solutions; uniform energy bounds; convergence for classical solutions.



1.
Introduction.

1.1.
Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann system

Two-species Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann system (in brief, VMB) describes the evolution of a gas of two species of oppositely charged particles (cations of charge
q^{+}>0
and mass
m^{+}>0
, and anions of charge
-q^{-}<0
and mass
m^{-}>0
), subject to auto-induced electromagnetic forces. Such a gas of charged particles, under a global neutrality condition, is called a plasma. The particle number densities
F^{+}(t,x,v)\geq 0
and
F^{-}(t,x,v)\geq 0
represent the distributions of the positively charged ions (i.e. cations), and the negatively charged ions (i.e. anions) at time
t\geq 0
, position
x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
, with velocity
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, respectively. Precisely, VMB system consists the following equations:

(1.1)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\partial_{t}F^{+}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F^{+}+\tfrac{q^{+}}{m^{+}}(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{+}=\mathcal{B}(F^{+},F^{+})+\mathcal{B}(F^{+},F^{-})\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}F^{-}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F^{-}-\tfrac{q^{-}}{m^{-}}(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{-}=\mathcal{B}(F^{-},F^{-})+\mathcal{B}(F^{-},F^{+})\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\mu_{0}\varepsilon_{0}\partial_{t}E-\nabla_{x}\times B=-\mu_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(q^{+}F^{+}-q^{-}F^{-})v\,\mathrm{d}v\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}B+\nabla_{\!x}\times E=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\mathrm{div}_{x}E=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(q^{+}F^{+}-q^{-}F^{-})\,\mathrm{d}v\,,\quad\!\mbox{and}\quad\!\mathrm{div}_{x}B=0\,.\end{array}\right.

The evolutions of the densities
F^{\pm}
are governed by the Vlasov-Boltzmann equations, which are the first two lines in (
1.1
). They tell that the variations of the densities
F^{\pm}
along the trajectories of the particles are subject to the influence of a Lorentz force and inter-particel collisions in the gas. The Lorentz force acting on the gas is auto-induced. That is, the electric field
E(t,x)
and the magnetic field
B(t,x)
are generated by the motion of the particles in the plasma itself. Their motion is governed by the Maxwell’s equations, which are the remaining equations in (
1.1
), namely Ampère equation, Faraday’s equation and Gauss’ laws respectively. In (
1.1
), the physical constants
\mu_{0},\varepsilon_{0}>0
are, respectively, the vacuum permeability (or magnetic constant) and the vacuum permittivity (or electric constant). Note that their relation to the speed of light is the formula
c=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}\varepsilon_{0}}}
. For the sake of mathematical convenience, we make the simplification that both kinds of particles have the same mass
m^{\pm}=m>0
and charge
q^{\pm}=q>0
.

The Boltzmann collision operator, presented in the right-hand sides of the Vlasov-Boltzmann equations in (
1.1
), is the quadratic form, acting on the velocity variable, associated to the bilinear operator,

\mathcal{B}(F,H)(v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(F^{\prime}H_{*}^{\prime}-FH_{*})b(v-v_{*},\cos\theta)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,,

where we have used the standard abbreviations

F=F(v)\,,\quad\!F^{\prime}=F(v^{\prime})\,,\quad\!H_{*}=H(v_{*})\,,\quad\!H^{\prime}_{*}=H(v^{\prime}_{*})\,,

with
(v^{\prime},v^{\prime}_{*})
given by

v^{\prime}=v-[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega\,,\quad\!v_{*}^{\prime}=v_{*}+[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega\,.

for
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
. In this paper, we will assume that the Boltzmann collision kernel is of the following
hard sphere
form

(1.2)

b(v-v_{*},\cos\theta)=|(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega|=|v-v_{*}||\cos\theta|\,.

This hypothesis is satisfied for all physical model and is more convenient to work with but do not impede the generality of our results. Then the collisional frequency can be defined as

(1.3)

\nu(v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|M(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,.

There have been extensive research on the well-posedness of the VMB. DiPerna-Lions developed a theory of global-in-time renormalized solutions with large initial data, in particular to the Boltzmann equation
[
20
]
, Vlasov-Maxwell equations
[
19
]
and Vlasov-Poisson-Boltzmann equation
[
48
,
49
]
. But for VMB there are severe difficulties, among which the major one is that the a priori bounds coming from physical laws are not enough to prove the existence of global solutions, even in the renormalized sense. Recently, Arsènio and Saint-Raymond
[
4
,
5
]
eventually established global-in-time renormalized solutions with large initial data for VMB, both cut-off and non-cutoff collision kernels. We emphasize that by far renormalized solutions are still the only existing theory for solutions without any smallness requirements on initial data. On the other line, in the context of classical solutions, through a so-called nonlinear energy method, Guo
[
31
]
constructed a classical solution of VMB near the global Maxwellian. Guo’s work inspired many results on VMB with more general collision kernels among which we only mention results for the most general collision kernels with or without angular cutoff assumptions, see
[
21
,
22
,
23
]
.

1.2.
Hydrodynamic limits

One of the most important features of the Boltzmann equations (or more generally, kinetic equations) is its connection to the fluid equations. The so-called fluid regimes of the Boltzmann equation are those of asymptotic dynamics of the scaled Boltzmann equations when the Knudsen number
\varepsilon
is very small. Justifying these limiting processes rigorously has been an active research field from late 70’s. Among many results obtained, the main contributions are the incompressible Navier-Stokes and Euler limits. There are two types of results in this field:

(1)

First obtaining the solutions of the scaled Boltzmann equation
uniform
in the Knudsen number
\varepsilon
, then extracting a convergent (at least weakly) subsequence converging to the solutions of the fluid equations as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0\,;
.

(2)

First obtaining the solutions for the limiting fluid equations, then constructing a sequence of special solutions (around the Maxwellian) of the scaled Boltzmann equations for small Knudsen number
\varepsilon
.

The key difference between the results of type (1) and (2) are: in type (1), the solutions of the fluid equations are
not
known a priori, and are completely obtained from taking limits from the Boltzmann equation. In short, it is “from kinetic to fluid”; In type (2), the solutions of the fluid equations are
known
first. In short, it is “from fluid to kinetic”.

The most successful program in type (1) is the so-called BGL program. As mentioned above, the DiPerna-Lions’s renormalized solutions for cutoff kernel
[
20
]
(also the non-cutoff kernels in
[
1
]
) are the only solutions known to exist globally without any restriction on the size of the initial data so far. From late 80’s, Bardos-Golse-Levermore initialized the program (BGL program in brief) to justify Leray’s solutions to the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations from DiPerna-Lions’ renormalized solutions
[
6
]
,
[
7
]
. They proved the first convergence result with 5 additional technical assumptions. After 10 years effects by Bardos, Golse, Levermore, Lions and Saint-Raymond, see for example
[
8
,
51
,
52
,
26
]
, the first complete convergence result without any additional compactness assumption was proved by Golse and Saint-Raymond in
[
27
]
for cutoff Maxwell collision kernel, and in
[
28
]
for hard cutoff potentials. Later on, it was extended by Levermore-Masmoudi
[
46
]
to include soft potentials. Recently Arsenio got the similar results for non-cutoff case
[
2
]
. Furthermore, by Jiang, Levermore, Masmoudi and Saint-Raymond, these results were extended to bounded domain where the Boltzmann equation was endowed with the Maxwell reflection boundary condition
[
54
,
40
,
42
]
, based on the solutions obtained by Mischler
[
55
]
.

The BGL program says that, given any
L^{2}\mbox{-}
bounded functions
(\rho_{0},\mathrm{u}_{0},\theta_{0})
, and for any physically bounded initial data (as required in DiPerna-Lions solutions)
F_{\varepsilon,0}=\mu+\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}g_{\varepsilon,0}
, such that suitable moments of the fluctuation
g_{\varepsilon,0}
, say,
(\mathcal{P}(g_{\varepsilon,0},v\sqrt{\mu})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},(g_{\varepsilon,0},(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{5}-1)\sqrt{\mu})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})})
converges in the sense of distributions to
(\mathrm{u}_{0},\theta_{0})
, the corresponding DiPerna-Lions solutions are
F_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)
. Then the fluctuations
g_{\varepsilon}
(defined by
F_{\varepsilon}=\mu+\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}g_{\varepsilon}
) has weak compactness, such that the corresponding moments of
g_{\varepsilon}
converge weakly in
L^{1}
to
(\mathrm{u},\theta)
which is a Leray solution of the incompressible Navier-Stokes equation whose viscosity and heat conductivity coefficients are determined by microscopic information, with initial data
(\mathrm{u}_{0},\theta_{0})
. Under some situations, for example the well-prepared initial data or in bounded domain with suitable boundary condition, the convergence could be strong
L^{1}
.

We emphasize that the BGL program indeed gave a new proof of Leray’s solutions to the incompressible Navier-Stokes equation, in particular the energy inequality which can be derived from the entropy inequality of the Boltzmann equation. Any a priori information of the Navier-Stokes equation is
not
needed, and completely derived from the microscopic Boltzmann equation. In this sense, BGL program is spiritually a part of Hilbert’s 6th problem: derive and justify the macroscopic fluid equations from the microscopic kinetic equations (see
[
57
]
).

Another direction in type (1) is in the context of classical solutions. The first work in this type is Bardos-Ukai
[
9
]
. They started from the scaled Boltzmann equation for cut-off hard potentials, and proved the global existence of classical solutions
g_{\varepsilon}
uniformly in
0<\varepsilon<1
. The key feature of Bardos-Ukai’s work is that they only need the smallness of the initial data, and did not assume the smallness of the Knudsen number
\varepsilon
. After having the uniform in
\varepsilon
solutions
g_{\varepsilon}
, taking limits can provide a classical solution of the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations with small initial data. Bardos-Ukai’s approach heavily depends on the sharp estimate especially the spectral analysis on the linearized Boltzmann operator
\mathcal{L}
, and the semigroup method (the semigroup generated by the scaled linear operator
\varepsilon^{-2}\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{-1}v\cdot\nabla_{\!x}
). It seems that it is hardly extended to soft potential cutoff, and even harder for the non-cutoff cases, since it is well-known that the operator
\mathcal{L}
has continuous spectrum in those cases. On the torus, semigroup approach has been employed by Briant
[
12
]
and Briant, Merino-Aceituno and Mouhot
[
15
]
to prove incompressible Navier-Stokes limit by employing the functional analysis breakthrough of Gualdani-Mischler-Mouhot
[
30
]
. Again, their results are for cut-off kernels with hard potentials. Recently, there is type (1) convergence result on the incompressible Navier-Stokes limit of the Boltzmann equation. In
[
44
]
, the uniform in
\varepsilon
global existence of the Boltzmann equation with or without cutoff assumption was obtained and the global energy estimates were established. Then taking limit as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, it was proved the incompressible Navier-Stokes limit.

Most of the type (2) results are based on the Hilbert expansion and obtained in the context of classical solutions. It was started from Nishida and Caflisch’s work on the compressible Euler limit
[
56
,
16
,
45
]
. Their approach was revisitied by Guo, Jang and Jiang, combining with nonlinear energy method to apply to the acoustic limit
[
33
,
34
,
38
]
. After then this process was used for the incompressible limits, for examples,
[
18
]
and
[
32
]
. In
[
18
]
, De Masi-Esposito-Lebowitz considered Navier-Stokes limit in dimension 2. More recently, using the nonlinear energy method, in
[
32
]
Guo justified the Navier-Stokes limit (and beyond, i.e. higher order terms in Hilbert expansion). This result was extended in
[
43
]
to more general initial data which allow the fast acoustic waves. These results basically say that, given the initial data which is needed in the classical solutions of the Navier-Stokes equation, it can be constructed the solutions of the Boltzmann equation of the form
F_{\varepsilon}=\mu+\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}(g_{1}+\varepsilon g_{2}+\cdots+\varepsilon^{n}g_{\varepsilon})
, where
g_{1},g_{2},\cdots
can be determined by the Hilbert expansion, and
g_{\varepsilon}
is the error term. In particular, the first order fluctuation
g_{1}=\rho_{1}+\mathrm{u}_{1}\!\cdot\!v+\theta_{1}(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\frac{3}{2})
, where
(\rho_{1},\mathrm{u}_{1},\theta_{1})
is the solutions to the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations.

1.3.
Hydrodynamic limits of Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann system

However, for the VMB, the corresponding hydrodynamic limits are much harder, even at the formal level, since it is coupled with Maxwell equations which are essentially hyperbolic. In a recent remarkable breakthrough
[
5
]
, Arsénio and Saint-Raymond not only proved the existence of renormalized solutions of VMB, as mentioned above, more importantly, also justified various limits (depending on the scalings) towards incompressible viscous electro-magneto-hydrodynamics. Among these limits, the most singular one is from renormalized solutions of two-species VMB to dissipative solutions of the two-fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell (in brief, NSFM) system with Ohm’s law.

The proofs in
[
5
]
for justifying the weak limit from a sequence of solutions of VMB to a dissipative solution of incompressible NSFM are extremely hard. Part of the reasons are, besides many difficulties of the existence of renormalized solutions of VMB itself, our current understanding for the incompressible NSFM with Ohm’s law is far from complete. From the point view of mathematical analysis, NSFM have a behavior which is more similar to the much less understood incompressible Euler equations than to the Navier-Stokes equations. That is the reason in
[
5
]
, they consider the so-called dissipative solutions of NSFM rather than the usual weak solutions. The dissipative solutions are were introduced by Lions for 3-dimensional incompressible Euler equations (see section 4.4 of
[
50
]
).

The studies of incompressible NSFM just started in recent years (for the introduction of physical background, see
[
10
,
17
]
). For weak solutions, the existence of global in time Leray type weak solutions are completely open, even in 2-dimension. A first breakthrough comes from Masmoudi
[
53
]
, who in 2-dimensional case proved the existence and uniqueness of global strong solutions of incompressible NSFM (in fact, the system he considered in
[
53
]
is little different with the NSFM in this paper, but the analytic analysis are basically the same) for the initial data
(v^{in},E^{in},B^{in})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times(H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))^{2}
with
s>0
. It is notable that in
[
53
]
, the divergence-free condition of the magnetic field
B
or the decay property of the linear part coming from Maxwell’s equations is
not
used. Ibrahim and Keraani
[
35
]
considered the data
(u^{in},E^{in},B^{in})\in\dot{B}^{1/2}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times(\dot{H}^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))^{2}
for 3-dimension, and
(v_{0},E_{0},B_{0})\in\dot{B}^{0}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times(L^{2}_{log}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))^{2}
for 2-dimensional case. Later on, German, Ibrahim and Masmoudi
[
24
]
refines the previous results by running a fixed-point argument to obtain mild solutions, but taking the initial velocity field in the natural Navier-Stokes space
H^{1/2}
. In their results the regularity of the initial velocity and electromagnetic fields is lowered. Furthermore, they employed an
L^{2}L^{\infty}
-estimate on the velocity field, which significantly simplifies the fixed-point arguments used in
[
35
]
. For some other asymptotic problems related, say, the derivation of the MHD from the Navier-Stokes-Maxwell system in the context of weak solutions, see Arsénio-Ibrahim-Masmoudi
[
3
]
. Recently, in
[
41
]
the authors of the current paper proved the global classical solutions of the incompressible NSFM with small intial data, by employing the decay properties of both the electric field and the wave equation with linear damping of the divergence free magnetic field. This key idea was already used in
[
24
]
.

The only previous hydrodynamic limit result for the VMB for classical solutions belong to Jang
[
37
]
. In fact, in
[
37
]
, it was taken a very special scaling that the magnetic effect appears only at a higher order. As a consequence, it vanishes in the limit as the Knudsen number
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. So in the limiting equations derived in
[
37
]
, there is no equation for the magnetic field at all. We emphasize that in
[
37
]
, the Hilbert expansion approach was employed, and the classical solutions to the VMB were constructed on those of the limiting equations. So the convergence results in
[
37
]
belong to the type (2) results, as we defined in the last subsection.

The main concern of the current paper is to prove a type (1) convergence result in the context of classical solutions for two-species VMB. In the scaling which will be specified in the below, we will establish a uniform in
\varepsilon
estimates for the VMB. As a consequence, we can establish the global in time existence of solutions of VMB near the Maxwellians uniform in
\varepsilon
. More over, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, the moments of the fluctuations converge to the classical solutions of the two-fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell (NSFM) system with Ohm’s law. We emphasize that our result belongs to type (1) result. So we do not need any a priori information of NSFM. In fact, our convergence result automatically provides a global existence of NSFM with Ohm’s law, of course, with small initial data.

We denote the Knudsen number by
\varepsilon
and the scaled two-species VMB reads

(1.4)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}\pm\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathcal{B}(F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm},F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm})+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathcal{B}(F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm},F_{\varepsilon}^{\mp})\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}E_{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B_{\varepsilon}=-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(F_{\varepsilon}^{+}-F_{\varepsilon}^{-})v\mathrm{d}v\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}B_{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E_{\varepsilon}=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\mathrm{div}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(F_{\varepsilon}^{+}-F_{\varepsilon}^{-})\mathrm{d}v\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\mathrm{div}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}=0\end{array}\right.

on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
Moreover, the initial data of (
1.4
) are imposed on

(1.5)

\displaystyle F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}(0,x,v)=F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm,in}(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}\,,\quad E_{\varepsilon}(0,x)=E_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,,\quad B_{\varepsilon}(0,x)=B_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,,

A physically relevant requirement for solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann system are their mass, momentum and energy are preserved with time. This is also an
a priori
property of the Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann system on the torus (see
[
5
]
for instance) which reads

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v(F_{\varepsilon}^{+}+F_{\varepsilon}^{-})\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v+\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}E_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}x\right)=0\,,

(1.6)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{2}(F_{\varepsilon}^{+}+F_{\varepsilon}^{-})\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(|E_{\varepsilon}|^{2}+|B_{\varepsilon}|^{2})\mathrm{d}x\right)=0\,.

Notice from the Maxwell system and the periodic boundary condition of
E_{\varepsilon}(t,x)
,

(1.7)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}B_{\varepsilon}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x=0\,.

We thus can define a constant vector

(1.8)

\tfrac{1}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}B_{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\bar{B}=\tfrac{1}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}B_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x)\mathrm{d}x\,.

It is well-known that the global equilibrium for the two-species Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann system is
([M(v),M(v)],0,\bar{B})
, where the normalized global
Maxwellian

M(v)
is

M(v)=\tfrac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\exp(-\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2})\,.

and
\bar{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is a constant vector. Our goal is to approximate the two-fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell system with Ohm’s law by the system (
1.4
) under the linearization
F_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}(t,x,v)=M(v)+\varepsilon\sqrt{M(v)}G_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}(t,x,v)
when the Knudsen number
\varepsilon
tends to zero. This leads to the perturbed two-species Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann system

(1.9)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}G_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big[v\cdot\nabla_{x}G_{\varepsilon}+\textsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}\big]G_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathscr{L}G_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)\sqrt{M}\textsf{q}_{1}\\[5.69054pt]
\qquad\qquad=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon},G_{\varepsilon})\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}E_{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B_{\varepsilon}=-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\textsf{q}_{1}v\sqrt{M}\mathrm{d}v\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}B_{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E_{\varepsilon}=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\mathrm{div}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\textsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\mathrm{d}v\,,\ \mathrm{div}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}=0\,,\end{array}\right.

where
G_{\varepsilon}=[G_{\varepsilon}^{+},G_{\varepsilon}^{-}]
represents the column vector in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with the components
G_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, the
2\times 2
diagonal matrix
\textsf{q}=\textit{diag}(1,-1)
, the column vector
\textsf{q}_{1}=[1,-1]
, the two species linearized collision operator
\mathscr{L}
is given as

(1.10)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}G_{\varepsilon}=\big[\mathcal{L}G_{\varepsilon}^{+}+\mathcal{L}(G_{\varepsilon}^{+},G_{\varepsilon}^{-})\,,\ \mathcal{L}G_{\varepsilon}^{-}+\mathcal{L}(G_{\varepsilon}^{-},G_{\varepsilon}^{+})\big]\,,

where

(1.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}g

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{M}}[\mathcal{B}(\sqrt{M}g,M)+\mathcal{B}(M,\sqrt{M}g)]

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{Q}(g,\sqrt{M})-\mathcal{Q}(\sqrt{M},g)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{M}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\tfrac{g}{\sqrt{M}}+\tfrac{g_{*}}{\sqrt{M_{*}}}-\tfrac{g^{\prime}}{\sqrt{M^{\prime}}}-\tfrac{g_{*}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{M_{*}^{\prime}}}\Big)|v-v_{*}|M_{*}\mathrm{d}v_{*}

is the usual linearized Boltzmann collision operator, and

(1.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(g,h)

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{M}}[\mathcal{B}(\sqrt{M}g,M)+\mathcal{B}(M,\sqrt{M}h)]

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{Q}(g,\sqrt{M})-\mathcal{Q}(\sqrt{M},h)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{M}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\tfrac{g}{\sqrt{M}}+\tfrac{h_{*}}{\sqrt{M_{*}}}-\tfrac{g^{\prime}}{\sqrt{M^{\prime}}}-\tfrac{h_{*}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{M_{*}^{\prime}}}\Big)|v-v_{*}|M_{*}\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,.

Here we denote by
\mathcal{Q}(g,h)=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{M}}\mathcal{B}(\sqrt{M}g,\sqrt{M}h)
. More specifically,

(1.13)

\mathcal{Q}(g,h)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(g^{\prime}h_{*}^{\prime}-gh_{*})b(|v-v_{*}|,\cos\theta)\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(g^{\prime}h_{*}^{\prime}-gh_{*})|v-v_{*}|\sqrt{M_{*}}\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,.

We then define a bilinear symmetric operator
\Gamma(G,H)
as

(1.14)

\displaystyle\Gamma(G,H)=\tfrac{1}{2}[\mathcal{Q}(G^{+},H^{+})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{Q}(H^{+},G^{+})+\mathcal{Q}(G^{+},H^{-})+\mathcal{Q}(H^{+},G^{-})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(G^{-},H^{-})+\mathcal{Q}(H^{-},G^{-})+\mathcal{Q}(G^{-},H^{+})+\mathcal{Q}(H^{-},G^{+})]

for vector-valued functions
G(v)=[G^{+}(v),G^{-}(v)]
and
H(v)=[H^{+}(v),H^{-}(v)]
.

The two species linearized collision operator
\mathscr{L}
has several properties (see
[
5
]
, for instance), which will be used throughout this paper. The linear operator
\mathscr{L}
is a closed self-adjoint operator in
L^{2}_{v}
with kernel

\textrm{Ker}(\mathscr{L})=\textrm{Span}\{\phi_{1}(v)\,,\cdots\phi_{6}(v)\}\,,

where
\phi_{1}(v)=[1,0]\sqrt{M}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathsf{q}_{1}+\mathsf{q}_{2})\sqrt{M}
,
\phi_{2}(v)=[0,1]\sqrt{M}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathsf{q}_{2}-\mathsf{q}_{1})\sqrt{M}
,
\phi_{i+2}(v)=[v_{i},v_{i}]\sqrt{M}=v_{i}\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}
for
i=1,2,3
and
\phi_{6}(v)=\tfrac{1}{2}[|v|^{2}-3,|v|^{2}-3]\sqrt{M}=(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}
. Here the column vectors
\mathsf{q}_{2}=[1,1]
. The family
(\phi_{i}(v))_{1\leq i\leq 6}
is an orthonormal basis of
\textrm{Ker}(\mathscr{L})
in
L^{2}_{v}
and we denote
\mathbb{P}
by the orthogonal projection onto
\textrm{Ker}(\mathscr{L})
in
L^{2}_{v}
:

(1.15)

\mathbb{P}G(v)=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\langle G,\phi_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\phi_{i}(v)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\phi_{i+2}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\phi_{i+2}(v)+\tfrac{1}{3}\left\langle G,\phi_{6}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\phi_{6}(v)

for all
G:\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{2}
in
L^{2}_{v}
. For notational simplicity, we denote by
\rho^{+}=\langle G,\phi_{1}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
,
\rho^{-}=\langle G,\phi_{2}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
,
u_{i}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\phi_{i+2}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
for
i=1,2,3
and
\theta=\tfrac{1}{3}\left\langle G,\phi_{6}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
, so that we can rewrite

(1.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}G=

\displaystyle\rho^{+}\phi_{1}(v)+\rho^{-}\phi_{2}(v)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}u_{i}\phi_{i+2}(v)+\theta\phi_{6}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{+}\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{1}+\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\sqrt{M}+\rho^{-}\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}-\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2}\sqrt{M}+u\cdot v\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}+\theta(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\rho+\tfrac{1}{2}n)\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{1}+\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\sqrt{M}+(\rho-\tfrac{1}{2}n)\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}-\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2}\sqrt{M}+u\cdot v\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}+\theta(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}\,,

where
\rho=\tfrac{1}{2}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})
and
n=\rho^{+}-\rho^{-}
. We also define
\mathbb{P}^{\perp}=\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P}
, where
\mathbb{I}
is the identical mapping.

For the usual linearized Boltzmann collision operator
\mathcal{L}
defined in (
1.11
), it is well known that the kernel of
\mathcal{L}
is

\textrm{Ker}(\mathcal{L})=\textrm{Span}\big\{\chi_{1}(v)\,,\ \chi_{2}(v)\,,\ \chi_{3}(v)\,,\ \chi_{4}(v)\,,\ \chi_{5}(v)\big\}\,,

where
\chi_{1}(v)=\sqrt{M(v)}
,
\chi_{1+i}(v)=v_{i}\sqrt{M(v)}
for
i=1,2,3
, and
\chi_{5}(v)=\big(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2}\big)\sqrt{M(v)}
. The collection
\big(\chi_{i}(v)\big)_{1\leq i\leq 5}
consists of an orthonormal basis of
\textrm{Ker}(\mathcal{L})
in
L^{2}_{v}
. One then can define the orthogonal projection
\Pi_{\mathcal{L}}
on to
\textrm{Ker}(\mathcal{L})
in
L^{2}_{v}
as

(1.17)

\Pi_{\mathcal{L}}g(v)=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\langle g,\chi_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\chi_{i}(v)+\tfrac{2}{3}\left\langle g,\chi_{5}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\chi_{5}(v)

for
g:\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
in
L^{2}_{v}
. We denote by
\Pi^{\perp}_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathbb{I}-\Pi_{\mathcal{L}}
. By direct verification, we know that for all
G=[G^{+},G^{-}]:\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{2}
in
L^{2}_{v}

(1.18)

\displaystyle\langle G^{+},\chi_{1}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=

\displaystyle\langle G,\phi_{1}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,(\mathsf{q}_{1}+\mathsf{q}_{2})\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,

\displaystyle\ \langle G^{-},\chi_{1}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=

\displaystyle\langle G,\phi_{2}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,(\mathsf{q}_{2}-\mathsf{q}_{1})\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,

\displaystyle\langle G^{\pm},\chi_{1+i}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\phi_{2+i}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\pm\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\textsf{q}\phi_{2+i}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\mathsf{q}_{2}v_{i}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\pm\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\mathsf{q}_{1}v_{i}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,\ (i=1,2,3)

\displaystyle\langle G^{\pm},\chi_{5}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{3}\langle G,\phi_{6}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\pm\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\textsf{q}\phi_{6}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\pm\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\mathsf{q}_{1}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,.

We denote briefly by

(1.19)

\displaystyle\rho^{+}=\left\langle G^{+},\chi_{1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\left\langle G,\phi_{1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,\ \rho^{-}=\left\langle G^{-},\chi_{1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\left\langle G,\phi_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,

\displaystyle u^{\pm}_{i}=\left\langle G^{\pm},\chi_{i+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\ \textrm{for }i=1,2,3,\ \theta^{\pm}=\left\langle G^{\pm},\tfrac{2}{3}\chi_{5}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,

\displaystyle u_{i}=\frac{1}{2}(u^{+}_{i}+u^{-}_{i})=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\phi_{2+i}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,\ \theta=\frac{1}{2}(\theta^{+}+\theta^{-})=\tfrac{1}{3}\langle G,\phi_{6}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

for all
G(x,v)=[G^{+}(x,v),G^{-}(x,v)]
belonging to
L^{2}_{x,v}
.

By assuming that the initial data
(F_{\varepsilon}^{in}=[F_{\varepsilon}^{+,in},F_{\varepsilon}^{-,in}],E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})
has the same mass, momentum and energy as the steady state
(M\mathsf{q}_{2},0,\bar{B})
, we can then rewrite the the conservation laws (
1.3
) in terms of the perturbation
(G_{\varepsilon}=[G_{\varepsilon}^{+},G_{\varepsilon}^{-}],E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
as

(1.20)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}G_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\cdot\phi_{i}(v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v=0\quad\textrm{for }i=1,2\,,\\[11.38109pt]
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}vG_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\cdot\ \textsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M(v)}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}E_{\varepsilon}(t,x)\times B_{\varepsilon}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x=0\,,\\[11.38109pt]
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}G_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\cdot\phi_{6}(v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(|E_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|^{2}+|B_{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\bar{B}|^{2})\mathrm{d}x=0\end{array}\right.

for all
t\geq 0
. Then, without loss of generality, the initial conditions of (
1.9
) shall be imposed on

(1.21)

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=G_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,,\quad E_{\varepsilon}(0,x)=E_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,,\quad B_{\varepsilon}(0,x)=B_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,,

which satisfy the conservation laws (
1.20
) and the constraint
\eqref{VMB-G}_{4}
initially.

1.4.
Notations.

We gather here the notations we will use throughout this paper. We first define the following shorthand notation,

\langle\cdot\rangle=\sqrt{1+|\cdot|^{2}}\,.

We denote the symbol
(f)_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}
by the average of the function
f(x)
over
x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
, namely,

(f)_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\overset{\Delta}{=}\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}f(x)\mathrm{d}x\,.

For convention, we index the usual
L^{p}
space by the name of the concerned variable. So we have, for
p\in[1,+\infty]
,

L^{p}_{[0,T]}=L^{p}([0,T])\,,\ L^{p}_{x}=L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\,,\ L^{p}_{v}=L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\,,\ L^{p}_{x,q}=L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})\,.

For
p=2
, we use the notations
\langle\cdot\,,\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}
,
\langle\cdot\,,\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
and
\langle\cdot\,,\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
to represent the inner product on the Hilbert spaces
L^{2}_{x}
,
L^{2}_{v}
and
L^{2}_{x,v}
, respectively.

Let
w:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a strictly positive measurable function. For
p\in[1,+\infty)
, we denote the
w
-weighted
L^{p}
spaces by

L^{p}_{v}(w)=L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3};w\mathrm{d}v)\,,\ L^{p}_{x,v}(w)=L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3};w\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x)

with the norms

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{p}_{v}(w)}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f(v)|^{p}w(v)\mathrm{d}v\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}<+\infty\,,

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{p}_{x,q}(w)}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|g(x,v)|^{p}w(v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}<+\infty\,.

For any multi-indexes
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3})
and
m=(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})
in
\mathbb{N}^{3}
we denote the
(m,\alpha)^{th}
partial derivative by

\partial^{m}_{\alpha}=\partial^{m}_{x}\partial^{\alpha}_{v}=\partial^{m_{1}}_{x_{1}}\partial^{m_{2}}_{x_{2}}\partial^{m_{3}}_{x_{3}}\partial^{\alpha_{1}}_{v_{1}}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{v_{2}}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{v_{3}}\,.

If each component of
m\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
is not greater than that of
\tilde{m}
’s, we denote by
m\leq\tilde{m}
. The symbol
m<\tilde{m}
means
m\leq\tilde{m}
and
|m|<|\tilde{m}|
, where
|m|=m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}
.

We define the spaces
H^{s}_{x,v}
and
H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
by the norms

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{s}_{x,q}}=\bigg(\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s}\|\partial_{\alpha}^{m}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,\ \|f\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}=\bigg(\sum_{|m|\leq s}\|\partial^{m}_{x}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Furthermore, we give the spaces
H^{s}_{x,v}(w)
and
H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(w)
with norms

\|f\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(w)}=\bigg(\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(w)}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,\ \|f\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(w)}=\bigg(\sum_{|m|\leq s}\|\partial^{m}_{x}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(w)}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

We also introduce the spaces
\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}
and
\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)
endowed with the norms

\|f\|_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}}=\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\\
|\alpha|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,\ \|f\|_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}=\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\\
|\alpha|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

1.5.
Main results.

There are two theorems built in this paper. The first theorem is about the global existence of the two-species VMB system uniform with respect to the Knudsen number
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. The second is on the two-fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell limit with Ohm’s law as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, taken from the solutions
(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
of the two-species VMB system (
1.9
) which are constructed in the first theorem.

To state our main theorems, we introduce the following energy functional and dissipation rate functional respectively

(1.22)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}(G,E,B)=

\displaystyle\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{s}(G,E,B)=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}B\|^{2}_{H^{s-2}_{x}}\,.

Theorem 1.1
.

For the integer
s\geq 3
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
, there are constants
\ell_{0}>0
,
c_{0}>0
and
c_{1}>0
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that if
\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{in},E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})\leq\ell_{0}
, then the Cauchy problem (
1.9
)-(
1.21
) admits a global solution

(1.23)

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\in L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v})\,,\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\in L^{2}_{t}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu))\,,

\displaystyle E_{\varepsilon}(t,x),B_{\varepsilon}(t,x)\in L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})

with the global uniform energy estimate

(1.24)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(t)+c_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(t)\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{1}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{in},E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})\,.

The next theorem is about the limit to the two fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell system with Ohm’s law:

(1.25)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla_{x}u-\mu\Delta_{x}u+\nabla_{x}p=\tfrac{1}{2}(nE+j\times B)\,,\qquad\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\theta-\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta=0\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \,\rho+\theta=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}E-\nabla_{x}\times B=-j\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \,\mathrm{div}_{x}\,E=n\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}B+\nabla_{x}\times E=0\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathrm{div}_{x}\,B=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\qquad\qquad j-nu=\sigma\big(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n+E+u\times B\big)\,,\qquad\quad\,\ w=\tfrac{3}{2}n\theta\,,\end{array}\right.

where the viscosity
\mu
, the heat conductivity
\kappa
and the electrical conductivity
\sigma
are given by

(1.26)

\displaystyle\mu=\tfrac{1}{10}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}A:\widehat{A}M\mathrm{d}v\,,\quad\kappa=\tfrac{2}{15}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}B\cdot\widehat{B}M\mathrm{d}v\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sigma=\tfrac{2}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Phi\cdot\widetilde{\Phi}M\mathrm{d}v\,.

For the derivation of (
1.26
), i.e. the relation of
\mu
,
\kappa
,
\sigma
with
A
,
\widehat{A}
,
B
,
\widehat{B}
,
\Phi
and
\widetilde{\Phi}
, see
[
5
]
.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,
s\geq 3
and
\ell_{0}>0
be as in Theorem
1.1
. Assume that the initial data
(G_{\varepsilon}^{in},E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})
in (
1.21
) satisfy

(1)

G_{\varepsilon}^{in}\in H^{s}_{x,v}
,
E_{\varepsilon}^{in}
,
B_{\varepsilon}^{in}\in H^{s}_{x}
;

(2)

\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{in},E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})\leq\ell_{0}
;

(3)

there exist scalar functions
\rho^{in}(x)
,
\theta^{in}(x)
,
n^{in}(x)\in H^{s}_{x}
and vector-valued functions
u^{in}(x)
,
E^{in}(x)
,
B^{in}(x)\in H^{s}_{x}
such that

(1.27)

\begin{array}[]{l}G_{\varepsilon}^{in}\rightarrow G^{in}\quad\textrm{strongly in}\ H^{s}_{x,v}\,,\\
E_{\varepsilon}^{in}\rightarrow E^{in}\quad\textrm{strongly in}\ H^{s}_{x}\,,\\
B_{\varepsilon}^{in}\rightarrow B^{in}\quad\textrm{strongly in}\ H^{s}_{x}\end{array}

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
G^{in}(x,v)
is of the form

(1.28)

\displaystyle G^{in}(x,v)=

\displaystyle(\rho^{in}(x)+\tfrac{1}{2}n^{in}(x))\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{1}+\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\sqrt{M}+(\rho^{in}(x)-\tfrac{1}{2}n^{in}(x))\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}-\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2}\sqrt{M}

\displaystyle+u^{in}\cdot v\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}+\theta^{in}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}\,.

Let
(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
be the family of solutions to the perturbed two-species Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann (
1.9
) with the initial conditions (
1.21
) constructed in Theorem
1.1
. Then, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
,

(1.29)

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow(\rho+\tfrac{1}{2}n)\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{1}+\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\sqrt{M}+(\rho-\tfrac{1}{2}n)\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}-\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2}\sqrt{M}+u\cdot v\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}+\theta(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}

weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
, strongly in
H^{s-1}_{x,v}
and weakly in
H^{s}_{x,v}
, and

(1.30)

\displaystyle E_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow E\quad\textrm{and }\quad B_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow B

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
, weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
and weakly in
H^{s}_{x}
. Here

(u,\theta,n,E,B)\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})

is the solution to the incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell equations (
1.25
) with Ohm’s law, which has the initial data

(1.31)

\displaystyle u|_{t=0}=\mathcal{P}u^{in}(x)\,,\ \theta|_{t=0}=\tfrac{3}{5}\theta^{in}(x)-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho^{in}(x)\,,\ E|_{t=0}=E^{in}(x)\,,\ B|_{t=0}=B^{in}(x)\,,

where
\mathcal{P}
is the Leray projection. Moreover, the convergence of the moments holds:

(1.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow u\,,

\displaystyle\langle G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{5}-1)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow\theta\,,

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
, weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
and weakly in
H^{s}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

The organization of this paper is as follows: in the next section, we give some basic properties of the linear collision operator and bilinear symmetric operator. In Section
3
, the spatial derivative estimates are derived, which are not closed. Then we derive the mixed derivative estimate and obtain a closed uniform energy inequality in Section
4
. Moreover, we also prove the global well-posedness under the small initial data for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. In Section
5
, based on the uniform global in time energy bound, we take the limit to derive the incompressible NSFM system with Ohm’s law. Finally, we construct the local classical solutions for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
under the small size of the initial data.

2.
Preliminaries

In this section we focus on some basic properties of the two species linearized collision operator
\mathscr{L}
and the bilinear symmetric operator
\Gamma
, which will be frequently used in the estimating of uniform energy bounds of the perturbed VMB system (
1.9
).

Lemma 2.1

(Collisional frequency)
.

The collision frequency
\nu(v)
defined in (
1.3
) has the following properties:

(1)

\nu(v)
is smooth and there are positive constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
such that

(2.1)

C_{1}(1+|v|)\leq\nu(v)\leq C_{2}(1+|v|)

for every
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

(2)

For any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
,
\alpha\neq 0
,

(2.2)

\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\partial^{\alpha}_{v}\nu(v)|<+\infty\,.

(3)

If the velocities
v
,
v_{*}
,
v^{\prime}
,
v_{*}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfy
v+v_{*}=v^{\prime}+v_{*}^{\prime}
and
|v|^{2}+|v_{*}|^{2}=|v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}
, then

(2.3)

\nu(v)+\nu(v_{*})\leq C_{3}(\nu(v^{\prime})+\nu(v_{*}^{\prime}))

holds for some positive constant
C_{3}
.

Proof of Lemma
2.1
.

We indeed can prove more general conclusions corresponding to Lemma
2.1
. More precisely, we consider
\nu(v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}M(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}
for any
\gamma\in[0,1]
, which is actually the collision frequency with respect to the hard potential (
\gamma\in(0,1]
) and Maxwellian (
\gamma=0
) collision kernel. In this case, the inequality (
2.1
) will be

(2.4)

C_{1}(1+|v|)^{\gamma}\leq\nu(v)\leq C_{2}(1+|v|)^{\gamma}\,,

and the last two conclusions in Lemma
2.1
are still valid.

We first prove the inequality (
2.4
). From the elementary bounds

(2.5)

|v-v_{*}|^{2}\leq(1+|v_{*}|^{2})(1+|v|^{2})\leq(1+|v_{*}|)^{2}(1+|v|)^{2}

for every
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and the fact
M(v_{*})=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}^{3}}e^{-\tfrac{|v_{*}|^{2}}{2}}>0
, we directly obtain the upper bound

\nu(v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}M(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|v_{*}|)^{\gamma}M(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}(1+|v|)^{\gamma}\,,

which yields the upper bound of (
2.4
).

Next, the bound (
2.5
) and the fact
M(v_{*})>0
imply that

(1+|v|)^{-\gamma}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}M(v_{*})\leq(1+|v_{*}|)^{\gamma}M(v_{*})\,.

Then the Lebesgue Dominated Convergence Theorem implies that the positive function

(2.6)

v\mapsto\frac{\nu(v)}{(1+|v|)^{\gamma}}=\frac{1}{(1+|v|)^{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}M(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}

is continuous over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and satisfies

\displaystyle\lim_{|v|\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\nu(v)}{(1+|v|)^{\gamma}}=

\displaystyle\lim_{|v|\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{(1+|v|)^{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}M(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}M(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}=1>0\,.

The function (
2.6
) is thereby bounded away from zero, thereby the lower bound of (
2.4
) follows.

We next derive the bound (
2.2
). One notices that

\displaystyle\nabla_{v}\nu(v)=

\displaystyle\gamma\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|^{2-\gamma}}M(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{u}{|u|^{2-\gamma}}M(v-u)\mathrm{d}u\,,

where the variables change
v_{*}\rightarrow u=v-v_{*}
is utilized. Then for any
\beta^{\prime}=[\beta_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{2}^{\prime},\beta_{3}^{\prime}]
,

(2.7)

\displaystyle|\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\nabla_{v}\nu(v)|=

\displaystyle\gamma\big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{u}{|u|^{2-\gamma}}\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}M(v-u)\mathrm{d}u\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\gamma\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{1}{|u|^{1-\gamma}}|\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}M(v-u)|\mathrm{d}u\,.

By direct calculations, we know that there is a constant
C_{\beta^{\prime}}>0
such that

|\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}M(v-u)|\leq C_{\beta^{\prime}}e^{-|v-u|^{2}/2}\,,

which implies that by (
2.7
)

\displaystyle|\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\nabla_{v}\nu(v)|\leq

\displaystyle C_{\beta^{\prime}}\gamma\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{1}{|u|^{1-\gamma}}e^{-\tfrac{|v-u|^{2}}{4}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma C_{\beta^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{1}{|v-v_{*}|^{1-\gamma}}e^{-\tfrac{|v_{*}|^{2}}{4}}\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma C_{\beta^{\prime}}\Big\{\int_{|v-v_{*}|\geq 1}+\int_{|v-v_{*}|<1}\Big\}\tfrac{1}{|v-v_{*}|^{1-\gamma}}e^{-\tfrac{|v_{*}|^{2}}{4}}\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\gamma C_{\beta^{\prime}}\int_{|v-v_{*}|\geq 1}e^{-\tfrac{|v_{*}|^{2}}{4}}\mathrm{d}v_{*}+\gamma C_{\beta^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\tfrac{1}{r^{1-\gamma}}r^{2}\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\gamma C_{\beta^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-\tfrac{|v_{*}|^{2}}{4}}\mathrm{d}v_{*}+\tfrac{4\pi\gamma}{2+\gamma}C_{\beta^{\prime}}<\infty\,.

Then the bound (
2.2
) holds.

Finally, we verify the inequality (
2.3
). By the elementary inequality

a^{2}+b^{2}\leq 2(a+b)^{2}\leq 4(a^{2}+b^{2})

for
a,b\geq 0
, we derive from the conditions of
v
,
v^{\prime}
,
v_{*}
and
v_{*}^{\prime}
in Lemma
2.1
(3) that

(2.8)

|v|+|v_{*}|\leq C(|v^{\prime}|+|v_{*}^{\prime}|)\,.

From the inequality (
2.1
), we have

\displaystyle\nu(v)+\nu(v_{*})\leq

\displaystyle\overline{C}\big[(1+|v|)^{\gamma}+(1+|v_{*}|)^{\gamma}\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\overline{C}\big[(1+|v|)+(1+|v_{*}|)\big]^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\gamma)\big[(1+|v^{\prime}|)+(1+|v_{*}^{\prime}|)\big]^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\gamma)\big[(1+|v^{\prime}|)^{\gamma}+(1+|v_{*}^{\prime}|)^{\gamma}\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C(\gamma)}{\underline{C}}(\nu(v^{\prime})+\nu(v_{*}^{\prime}))\,,

where the elementary inequality

(a+b)^{\gamma}\leq a^{\gamma}+b^{\gamma}\ (a,b\geq 0\,,\gamma\in[0,1])

is used. Then the proof of Lemma
2.1
is finished.
∎

Lemma 2.2

(Control of
\nu
-weighted norms)
.

The related
\nu
-weighted norms have the following properties:

(1)

There is a constant
C_{4}>0
, such that for all
h\in L^{2}_{v}(\nu)
,

(2.9)

\|h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\leq C_{4}\|h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\,.

(2)

Let the integer
s\geq 1
. Then there exist positive constants
C_{5}
and
C_{6}
, such that for all
h\in H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)

(2.10)

\Big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}(\nu(v)h)\,,\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\geq C_{5}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}-C_{6}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

holds for
|m|+|\alpha|=s
with
|\alpha|\geq 1
.

Proof of Lemma
2.2
.

(1) From the inequality (
2.1
), we derive that

(2.11)

\displaystyle C_{1}\leq\nu(v)\,,

which immediately implies the inequality (
2.9
) holds.

(2) Via direct calculation, we obtain

(2.12)

\displaystyle\big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}(\nu(v)h),\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=

\displaystyle\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\underset{K}{\underbrace{\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{v}\nu(v)\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}h,\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}\,.

Then, we employ the Hölder inequality, the part (2) of Lemma
2.1
, the part (1) of Lemma
2.2
and the Young’s inequality to estimate the term
K
in the previous equality (
2.12
). More precisely, we have

(2.13)

\displaystyle|K|\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha}\|\partial^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{v}\nu(v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}h\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}h\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

Substituting the inequality (
2.13
) into (
2.12
) implies that

(2.14)

\displaystyle\big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}(\nu(v)h),\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}-C\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,.

Thus the proof of Lemma
2.2
is finished.
∎

Lemma 2.3

(Coercivity on
\mathscr{L}
)
.

The two species linearized collision operator
\mathscr{L}:L^{2}_{v}\rightarrow L^{2}_{v}
has the following properties:

(1)

\mathscr{L}
is closed, self-adjoint and can be decomposed as

\mathscr{L}=2\nu(v)\mathbb{I}-\mathscr{K},

where
\mathscr{K}
is a compact operator in
L^{2}_{v}
, and
\nu(v)
is the collisional frequency.

(2)

Let the integer
s\geq 1
. Then for any
\delta>0
, there is a
C(\delta)>0
such that for all
h\in H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)

(2.15)

\Big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathscr{K}(h),\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C(\delta)\|\partial^{m}_{x}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\delta\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

holds for
|m|+|\alpha|=s
,
|\alpha|\geq 1
.

(3)

There is a
\lambda>0
, such that for all
h\in L^{2}_{v}

(2.16)

\langle\mathscr{L}h,h\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\geq\lambda\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}h\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\,.

Proof of Lemma
2.3
.

(1) The proof can be referred to Proposition 5.6 of
[
5
]
or
[
47
]
for details.

(2) We refer to Lemma 2 of
[
31
]
for details fo proof, and we omit the details here.

(3) The coercivity of
\mathscr{L}
has been proved in Proposition 5.7 of
[
5
]
or Lemma 1 of
[
31
]
. We omit the details here.
∎

Lemma 2.4

(Control of
\Gamma
: Torus version)
.

Let
\Gamma:L^{2}_{v}\times L^{2}_{v}\rightarrow L^{2}_{v}
be the bilinear symmetric operator defined in (
1.14
).

(1)

For any
G,H\in L^{2}_{v}
, we have

(2.17)

\Gamma(G,H)\in\mathrm{Ker}^{\perp}(\mathscr{L})\,.

(2)

Let any integer
s\geq 3
and
F\in L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)
,
G,H\in H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)
. Then for all
m,\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
,
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
,

(2.18)

\bigg|\Big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\Gamma(G,H),F\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\bigg|\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{G}^{s}_{x,v}(G,H)\|F\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,\quad\textrm{if }\alpha\neq 0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\mathcal{G}^{s}_{x}(G,H)\|F\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,\quad\ \textrm{if }\alpha=0\,,\end{array}\right.

where
\mathcal{G}^{s}_{x,v}
and
\mathcal{G}^{s}_{x}
satisfy
\mathcal{G}^{s}_{x,v}\leq\mathcal{G}^{s+1}_{x,v}
,
\mathcal{G}^{s}_{x}\leq\mathcal{G}^{s+1}_{x}
and there exists a positive constant
C_{\Gamma}>0
such that

(2.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{s}_{x,v}(G,H)

\displaystyle=C_{\Gamma}\left(\|G\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|H\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\|G\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|H\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\right)\,,

and

(2.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{s}_{x}(G,H)

\displaystyle=C_{\Gamma}\left(\|G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|H\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\|G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|H\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\right)\,.

Proof of Lemma
2.4
.

(1) Since
\mathcal{Q}(g,h)\in\textrm{Ker}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L})
, we easily verify that
\Gamma(G,H)\in\textrm{Ker}^{\perp}(\mathscr{L})
.

(2) We first estimate the term
\big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(g_{1},g_{2}),g_{3}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
for all
g_{1},g_{2}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
in
H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)
and
g_{3}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
in
L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)
. Straightforward calculations give us

(2.21)

\displaystyle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(g_{1},g_{2})=\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(g_{1}(v^{\prime})g_{2}(v_{*}^{\prime})-g_{1}(v)g_{2}(v_{*})\big)|v-v_{*}|\sqrt{M(v_{*})}\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle=\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big[g_{1}(v-\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)g_{2}(v_{*}+\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)

\displaystyle-g_{1}(v)g_{2}(v_{*})\big]|v-v_{*}|M^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}^{u=v-v_{*}}}\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big[g_{1}(v-\sigma\cdot u\sigma)g_{2}(v-u+\sigma\cdot u\sigma)

\displaystyle-g_{1}(v)g_{2}(v-u)\big]|u|M^{\frac{1}{2}}(v-u)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{gain}}{\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}=\alpha\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(v-\sigma\cdot u\sigma)\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v-u+\sigma\cdot u\sigma)|u|\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{v}M^{\frac{1}{2}}(v-u)\mathrm{d}u}}

\displaystyle\underset{I_{loss}}{\underbrace{-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}=\alpha\end{subarray}}\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v-u)|u|\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{v}M^{\frac{1}{2}}(v-u)\mathrm{d}u}}\,.

Estimates on
I_{loss}
.
It is easy to know that for any
\eta\in(0,1)
and
\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{3}

(2.22)

\partial^{\beta}_{v}M^{\frac{1}{2}}(v-u)\leq CM^{\frac{\eta}{2}}(v-u)

holds for some positive constant
C
. Then the second term
I_{loss}
is bounded by

(2.23)

\displaystyle|I_{loss}|

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\big|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{v}(v)\big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|M^{\frac{\eta}{2}}(v-u)\big|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v-u)\big|\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\big|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{v}(v)\big|\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{2}M^{\eta}(v-u)\mathrm{d}u\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v-u)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}u\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

The following elementary inequalities

|v-v_{*}|^{2}\leq(1+|v_{*}|^{2})(1+|v|^{2})\leq(1+|v_{*}|)^{2}(1+|v|)^{2}

and Lemma
2.1
(1) yield that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{2}M^{\eta}(v-u)\mathrm{d}u=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{2}M^{\eta}(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+|v|)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|v_{*}|)^{2}M^{\eta}(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}\leq C\nu^{2}(v)\,.

Then we have

\displaystyle|I_{loss}|\leq C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\nu(v)\big|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(v)\big|\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v-u)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}u\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

which immediately derive from the Hölder inequality that

(2.24)

\displaystyle\Big|\big\langle I_{loss},g_{3}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\Big\langle|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(v)|\nu(v)\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{v}},g_{3}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\underset{{\bf I}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}^{m_{1},m_{2}}}{\underbrace{\Big\langle\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{v}},\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}}}\,.

We next control the terms
{\bf I}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
for all multi-indexes
m_{1}
,
m_{2}
,
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
in
\mathbb{N}^{3}
satisfying
m_{1}|+|m_{2}=m
and
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha
. If
\alpha=0
and
m_{1}=0
or
m_{2}=0
, via using the Hölder inequality and the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
, we have

(2.25)

\displaystyle\big|{\bf I}^{m,0}_{0,0}\big|+\big|{\bf I}^{0,m}_{0,0}\big|\leq

\displaystyle\big(\|\partial^{m}_{x}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|g_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m}_{x}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big)\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\partial^{m}_{x}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|g_{1}\|_{H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m}_{x}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big)\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

If
\alpha=0
and
m_{1},m_{2}\neq 0
, the Hölder inequality and the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
imply that

(2.26)

\displaystyle{\bf I}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{0,0}\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{x}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\big\|_{L^{4}_{x}}\big\|\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{x}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\|_{L^{4}_{x}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big\|\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{x}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\big\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\big\|\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{x}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,

where the last inequality is derived from the inequality

(2.27)

\big\|\nabla_{x}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{v}(w)}\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\|\nabla_{x}g\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(w)}

for
w(v)=1
or
\nu(v)
. This inequality is derived from the Hölder inequality as follows:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{x}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{v}(w)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Big|\nabla_{x}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{v}(w)}\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Big|\tfrac{\nabla_{x}\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(w)}}{2\|g\|_{L^{2}_{v}(w)}}\Big|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Big|\tfrac{\nabla_{x}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|g|^{2}w\mathrm{d}v}{2\|g\|_{L^{2}_{v}(w)}}\Big|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Big|\tfrac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g\nabla_{x}gw\mathrm{d}v}{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{v}(w)}}\Big|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla_{x}g|^{2}w\mathrm{d}vdx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\|\nabla_{x}g\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(w)}\,.

If
\alpha\neq 0
and
m_{1}+\alpha_{1}=0
or
m_{2}+\alpha_{2}=0
, then one derives from the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
that

(2.28)

\displaystyle\big|{\bf I}^{0,m}_{0,\alpha_{2}}\big|+\big|{\bf I}^{m,0}_{\alpha_{1},0}\big|\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\|g_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\Big)\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\|g_{1}\|_{H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\Big)\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

Here we require
s\geq 2
. If
\alpha\neq 0
and
m_{1}+\alpha_{1}\neq 0
,
m_{2}+\alpha_{2}\neq 0
, then the terms
{\bf I}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
are bounded by

(2.29)

\displaystyle{\bf I}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\big\|_{L^{4}_{x}}\big\|\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\|_{L^{4}_{x}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big\|\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\big\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\big\|\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big\|g_{1}\big\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big\|g_{2}\big\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big\|g_{1}\big\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big\|g_{2}\big\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

Here the Hölder inequality, the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
and the inequality (
2.27
) are utilized. We summarize the all above bounds on the terms
{\bf I}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
and obtain

(2.30)

\displaystyle\Big|\big\langle I_{loss},g_{3}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\Big|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}{\bf I}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}C\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\qquad\textrm{if }\alpha\neq 0\,,\\[11.38109pt]
C\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\quad\ \textrm{if }\alpha=0\,.\end{array}\right.

Estimates on
I_{gain}
.
We next deal with the term
I_{gain}
. By the Hölder inequality and the part (3) of Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{gain}|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}=\alpha\end{subarray}}\left(|u|^{2\gamma}|\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{v}M^{\frac{1}{2}}(v-u)|^{2}\mathrm{d}u\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(v-\sigma\cdot u\sigma)|^{2}|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v-u+\sigma\cdot u\sigma)|^{2}\mathrm{d}u\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\nu(v)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(v-\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)|^{2}|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v_{*}+\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)|^{2}\mathrm{d}u\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big[\nu(v-\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)+\nu(v_{*}+\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\times|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(v-\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)|^{2}|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v_{*}+\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{*}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

which implies that

\displaystyle\Big|

\displaystyle\langle I_{gain},g_{3}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\Big|\leq C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\Bigg\langle\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big[\nu(v-\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)+\nu(v_{*}+\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\times|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(v-\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)|^{2}|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v_{*}+\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{*}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,g_{3}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)\Bigg\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big[\nu(v-\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)+\nu(v_{*}+\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(v-\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)|^{2}|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(v_{*}+\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{*}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{w=v-\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma}_{w_{*}=v_{*}+\sigma\cdot(v-v_{*})\sigma}}C\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\nu(w)+\nu(w_{*})]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}(w)|^{2}|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}(w_{*})|^{2}\mathrm{d}w_{*}\mathrm{d}w\mathrm{d}x\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=C\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\Bigg(\underset{\textbf{II}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}}{\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\mathrm{d}x}}

(2.31)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\underset{\textbf{III}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}}{\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\mathrm{d}x}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

We will estimate term
\textbf{II}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
in the previous equality by employing the Hölder inequality, the Sobolev embeddings
H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{4}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
and
H^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
.

If
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
\alpha\neq 0
and the multi-indexes
m_{1}
,
m_{2}
,
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
in
\mathbb{N}^{3}
satisfy
|m_{1}|+|\alpha_{1}|\leq s-1
,
|m_{2}|+|\alpha_{2}|\leq s-1
and
m_{1}+m_{2}=m
,
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha
, we deduce that

(2.32)

\displaystyle\textbf{II}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\big\|^{2}_{L^{4}_{x}}\big\|\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\|^{2}_{L^{4}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|g_{1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\,,

where the inequality (
2.27
) is utilized. If
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
and
\alpha\neq 0
satisfying
|m_{1}|+|\alpha_{1}|=s
or
|m_{2}|+|\alpha_{2}|=s
, then
m_{2}=\alpha_{2}=0
or
m_{1}=\alpha_{1}=0
, respectively. Then we can estimate that

(2.33)

\displaystyle\textbf{II}^{0,m_{2}}_{0,\alpha_{2}}+\textbf{II}^{m_{1},0}_{\alpha_{1},0}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\|g_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial^{m_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}g_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|g_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}g_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|g_{1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\,.

Here the condition
s\geq 2
is required. In summary, we obtain that for
m_{1}+m_{2}=m
and
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha
with
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\,(\alpha\neq 0)

(2.34)

\textbf{II}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\leq C\|g_{1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\,.

Furthermore, the term
\textbf{III}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
can be controlled by borrowing the analogous arguments of the estimates on the term
\textbf{II}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
in (
2.34
). More precisely,

(2.35)

\displaystyle\textbf{III}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\leq C\|g_{1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|g_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}

holds for all
m_{1}+m_{2}=m
and
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha
with
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\,(\alpha\neq 0)
. We thereby derive from plugging the bounds (
2.34
) and (
2.35
) into the relation (
2
) that

(2.36)

\displaystyle\big|\langle I_{gain},g_{3}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big|\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m\\
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\big(\textbf{II}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}+\textbf{III}^{m_{1},m_{2}}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}+\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big)\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

holds for all
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
\alpha\neq 0
. Via the similar arguments of (
2.36
), one can also yield that

(2.37)

\displaystyle\big|\langle I_{gain},g_{3}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big|\leq C\big(\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\big)\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

hold for all
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
\alpha=0
.

Combining the bounds (
2.30
), (
2.36
) and (
2.37
), we derive from (
2.21
) that

(2.38)

\displaystyle\big|\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(g_{1},g_{2}),g_{3}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big|\leq\big|\langle I_{loss},g_{3}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big|+\big|\langle I_{gain},g_{3}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}C\big(\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\big)\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,\quad\textrm{if }\ \alpha=0\\[5.69054pt]
C\big(\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}+\|g_{1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|g_{2}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big)\|g_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,\quad\quad\ \,\textrm{if }\ \alpha\neq 0\,.\end{array}\right.

holds for all
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
. Here
s\geq 2
is required.

We finally estimate the quantity
\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\Gamma(G,H),F\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
for
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
. Recalling the definition of
\Gamma(G,H)
in (
1.14
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\Gamma(G,H)=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}[\mathcal{Q}(G^{+},H^{+})+\mathcal{Q}(H^{+},G^{+})+\mathcal{Q}(G^{+},H^{-})+\mathcal{Q}(H^{+},G^{-})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(G^{-},H^{-})+\mathcal{Q}(H^{-},G^{-})+\mathcal{Q}(G^{-},H^{+})+\mathcal{Q}(H^{-},G^{+})]\,,

we have

(2.39)

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\Gamma(G,H),F\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(G^{+},H^{+}),F^{+}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(H^{+},G^{+}),F^{+}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(G^{-},H^{-}),F^{-}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(H^{-},G^{-}),F^{-}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(G^{+},H^{-}),F^{+}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(H^{+},G^{-}),F^{+}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(G^{-},H^{+}),F^{-}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(H^{-},G^{+}),F^{-}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\tau=\pm}\sum_{\gamma=\pm}\big[\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(G^{\tau},H^{\gamma}),F^{\tau}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(H^{\tau},G^{\gamma}),F^{\tau}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big]\,.

Then the inequalities (
2.38
) imply that

(2.40)

\displaystyle\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\Gamma(G,

\displaystyle H),F\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{\tau=\pm}\sum_{\gamma=\pm}\big(\|G^{\tau}\|_{X_{\alpha}(\nu)}\|H^{\gamma}\|_{X_{\alpha}}+\|G^{\tau}\|_{X_{\alpha}}\|H^{\gamma}\|_{X_{\alpha}(\nu)}\big)\|F^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+C\sum_{\tau=\pm}\sum_{\gamma=\pm}\big(\|H^{\tau}\|_{X_{\alpha}(\nu)}\|G^{\gamma}\|_{X_{\alpha}}+\|H^{\tau}\|_{X_{\alpha}}\|G^{\gamma}\|_{X_{\alpha}(\nu)}\big)\|F^{\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|G\|_{X_{\alpha}(\nu)}\|H\|_{X_{\alpha}}+\|G\|_{X_{\alpha}}\|H\|_{X_{\alpha}(\nu)}\big)\|F\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

holds for all
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
, where
X_{\alpha}=H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
if
\alpha=0
, while
X_{\alpha}=H^{s}_{x,v}
if
\alpha\neq 0
. Here the facts
\|G^{\pm}\|_{Y}\leq C\|G\|_{Y}

(Y=X_{\alpha},X_{\alpha}(\nu),L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu))
is also utilized. We thus complete the proof of Lemma
2.4
.
∎

3.
Uniform energy estimates on the spatial derivatives.

At beginning of this section, we give the following local well-posedness of the perturbed VMB system (
1.9
) with small initial data:

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
and
s\geq 3
. There are
\ell>0
and
T^{*}>0
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that if
\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{in},E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})\leq\ell
and
T^{*}\leq\sqrt{\ell}
, the Cauchy problem (
1.9
)-(
1.21
) admits a unique solution
(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
satisfying

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(0,T^{*};H^{s}_{x,v}),E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(0,T^{*};H^{s}_{x})

with the energy bound

(3.1)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\mathrm{d}t\leq C\ell\,,

where the constant
C>0
is independent of
\varepsilon
.

The proof of Proposition
3.1
will be given in Section Appendix
A
. Our goal of this section is to derive the uniform energy estimates on the spatial derivatives to the perturbed VMB system (
1.9
). For notational simplicity, we drop the lower index
\varepsilon
, i.e.,

(3.2)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}G+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big[v\cdot\nabla_{x}G+\textsf{q}(\varepsilon E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}\big]G+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathscr{L}G-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(E\cdot v)\sqrt{M}\textsf{q}_{1}\\[5.69054pt]
\qquad\qquad=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}(E\cdot v)G+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(G,G)\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}E-\nabla_{x}\times B=-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G\cdot\textsf{q}_{1}v\sqrt{M}\mathrm{d}v\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}B+\nabla_{x}\times E=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\mathrm{div}_{x}E=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G\cdot\textsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\mathrm{d}v\,,\ \mathrm{div}_{x}B=0\,.\end{array}\right.

The
key points
are the following three aspects: First, the two species linearized collision operator part
\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathscr{L}G
will give us kinetic dissipation term
\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}
with singularity
\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}
. Secondly, by the
micro-macro decomposition
, we can obtain a fluid dissipation term
\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
. Finally, we find the construction of Ohm’s law, which will gives us a damping term
\partial_{t}B
of the Farady equation, so that we can get the global energy estimate on the electric field
E
and magnetic field
B
.

3.1.
Energy estimates with kinetic dissipation.

In this subsection, we will give the energy estimates of the spatial derivatives with kinetic dissipation by direct energy methods. More precisely, we prove the following proposition.

Proposition 3.2
.

Assume that
(G,E,B)
is the solution to the perturbed VMB system (
1.9
) constructed in Proposition
3.1
. Let the integer
s\geq 3
. Then there are constants
\lambda>0
and
C>0
, independent of
\varepsilon>0
, such that

(3.3)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\right)+\tfrac{\lambda}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|E\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\left(\|\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C\|E\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\Big(\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\sum_{|m|\leq s-1}\|\nabla_{v}\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\left(\|G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|B\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right)\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\|\mathbb{P}G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\sum_{|m|\leq s-1}\|\nabla_{v}\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big)\,.

Proof of Proposition
3.2
.

For all multi-index
m\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
satisfying
|m|\leq s
(
s\geq 3
), we act
\partial^{m}_{x}
on the first equation of (
3.2
) and take the
L^{2}_{x,v}
-inner product with
\partial^{m}_{x}G
. Then we have

(3.4)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}_{x}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\langle\mathscr{L}\partial^{m}_{x}G,\partial^{m}_{x}G\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle(\partial^{m}_{x}E\cdot v)\sqrt{M}\mathsf{q}_{1},\partial^{m}_{x}G\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m}_{x}[(E\cdot v)G],\partial^{m}_{x}G\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{x}\Gamma(G,G),\partial^{m}_{x}G\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}\left\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(\varepsilon E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}G,\partial^{m}_{x}G\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,.

First, from Part (3) of Lemma
2.3
, we know that there is a
\lambda>0
such that

(3.5)

\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\langle\mathscr{L}\partial^{m}_{x}G,\partial^{m}_{x}G\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\geq\tfrac{\lambda}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,
