\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\sum_{m^{\prime}\leq m}\|\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}B\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|B\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

Substituting the bounds (
4.49
), (
4.50
) and (
4.51
) into the equality (
4.48
), we obtain that

(4.52)

\displaystyle M_{9}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+C_{\gamma}\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\big(\varepsilon\|E\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

holds for all
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
and
\alpha\neq 0
, where
\gamma>0
is small to be determined.

Finally, we plug the bounds (
4.9
), (
4.10
), (
4.18
), (
4.21
), (
4.22
), (
4.23
), (
4.30
), (
4.47
) and (
4.52
) into the equality (
4.5
) and then obtain

(4.53)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{\lambda_{0}-4\gamma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{\lambda_{1}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+C_{\gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq s\\
\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{\gamma}\big(\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+2\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}(1+\|E\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|_{H^{s}_{x}})(\|\mathbb{P}G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}})

\displaystyle\times(\|E\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}B\|_{H^{s-2}_{x}})\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\big(\varepsilon\|E\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbb{P}G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\big)

\displaystyle\times\big(\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big)\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
\alpha\neq 0
and for small
\gamma>0
to be determined. We take
\gamma=\tfrac{1}{16}\lambda_{0}>0
and then we derive from the previous bound (
4.53
) and the Young’s inequality that

(4.54)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{\lambda_{0}}{2\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq s\\
\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+C\big(\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C(1+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}})(\|\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}})(\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}B\|^{2}_{H^{s-2}_{x}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C\big(\varepsilon^{2}\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\big)

\displaystyle\times\big(\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big)

for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
and
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
\alpha\neq 0
.

Recalling the definitions of the energy functionals
\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)
and
\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)
in (
3.190
), one can obtain that the following inequalities

(4.55)

\displaystyle(1+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}})(\|\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}})

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B))(\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}})\,,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\leq C(\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}})\,,

\displaystyle\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}B\|^{2}_{H^{s-2}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|^{2}_{H^{s-2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\leq C(1+\tfrac{1}{\eta})\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)

hold for all
0<\eta\leq\eta_{0}
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Then the relations (
4.54
) and (
4.55
) reduce to

(4.56)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{\lambda_{0}}{2\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq s\\
\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+C(1+\tfrac{1}{\eta})\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C(1+\tfrac{1}{\eta})(1+\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B))(\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}})\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)

for all
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
\alpha\neq 0
and for any
0<\eta\leq\eta_{0}
,
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.

Noticing that the energy functional
\mathscr{D}_{w}(G)
defined in (
3.190
) can be dominated by

(4.57)

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{w}(G)\leq\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,

we derive from multiplying the inequality (
4.56
) by
\tfrac{\eta}{2C(1+\eta)}
and adding it to the inequality (
3.194
) that

(4.58)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big\{\tfrac{1}{2}\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\varepsilon\eta\mathscr{A}_{s}(G,E,B)+\tfrac{\eta}{4C(1+\eta)}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\Big\}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{\eta}{4C(1+\eta)}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{\eta}{2(1+\eta)}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq s\\
\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+C\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G,E,B)\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\big(1+\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)\big)\big(\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big)\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)

\displaystyle+C(1+\tfrac{1}{\eta})\big[\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G,E,B)+\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{2}(G,E,B)\big]\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)

holds for all
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
\alpha\neq 0
and for any
0<\eta\leq\eta_{0}
,
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.

We observe that the previous energy inequality (
4.58
) is not closed, since the first term
\tfrac{\eta}{2(1+\eta)}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq s\\
\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}
in the right-hand side of (
4.58
) is uncontrolled. One notices that the highest oder of
v
-derivatives in that term is less that
|\alpha|=k
, which inspires us that we can employ an induction over the
|\alpha|=k
, the order of
v
-derivative, to prove the energy bound (
4.2
).

For
k=0
, the energy bound (
3.194
) in Proposition
3.6
and the inequality (
4.57
) imply that (
4.2
) holds. Now we assume the lemma is valid for
k
. For
|\alpha|=k+1
, summing up for
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
|\alpha|=k+1
in the inequality (
4.58
), we obtain

(4.59)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\bigg\{\tfrac{1}{2}N_{k+1}\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+N_{k+1}\varepsilon\eta\mathscr{A}_{s}(G,E,B)

\displaystyle+\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s,|\alpha|=k+1}\tfrac{\eta}{4C(1+\eta)}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{\eta}{2(1+\eta)}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s,|\alpha|=k+1}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\tfrac{1}{2}N_{k+1}\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{N_{k+1}\eta}{2(1+\eta)}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s,|\alpha|\leq k}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+CN_{k+1}\Big\{\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G,E,B)\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+\big(1+\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)\big)\big(\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big)

\displaystyle+(1+\tfrac{1}{\eta})\big[\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G,E,B)+\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{2}(G,E,B)\big]\Big\}\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)\,.

Here
N_{k+1}>0
denotes the number of all possible
(m,\alpha)
such that
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
,
|\alpha|=k+1
. By the assumption of the induction, (
4.2
) is valid for the case
k
. In order to absorb the first term on the right-hand side in (
4.59
) by the last second term on the left-hand side of (
4.2
), we multiply (
4.59
) by
\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{N_{k+1}}
and add it to (
4.2
). We then get

(4.60)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\bigg\{(\varrho_{k}+\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{2})\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s,|\alpha|\leq k}\tfrac{C_{|\alpha|}\eta}{1+\eta}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+(\varrho_{k}^{*}+\delta_{k})\varepsilon\eta\mathscr{A}_{s}(G,E,B)+\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s,|\alpha|=k+1}\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{4CN_{k+1}}\tfrac{\eta}{1+\eta}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+(\delta_{k}^{*}+\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{2})\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\tfrac{\delta_{k}\eta}{2(1+\eta)}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s,|\alpha|\leq k}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{N_{k+1}}\tfrac{\eta}{2(1+\eta)}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s,|\alpha|=k+1}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(C_{k}^{*}+C\delta_{k})\Big\{\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G,E,B)\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}+(1+\tfrac{1}{\eta})\big[\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G,E,B)+\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{2}(G,E,B)\big]

\displaystyle+(1+\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B))\big(\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big)\Big\}\mathscr{D}_{\eta}(G,E,B)\,.

We thereby conclude our lemma from the previous inequality (
4.60
) by letting

\displaystyle C_{k+1}=\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{4CN_{k+1}}\,,\qquad\qquad\!C^{*}_{k+1}=C_{k}^{*}+C\delta_{k}\,,

\displaystyle\varrho_{k+1}=\varrho_{k}+\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{2}\,,\qquad\qquad\varrho_{k+1}^{*}=\varrho_{k}^{*}+\delta_{k}\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{k+1}=\min\big\{\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{2},\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{2N_{k+1}}\big\}\,,\ \delta_{k+1}^{*}=\delta_{k}^{*}+\tfrac{\delta_{k}}{2}\,.

∎

Next we derive a closed energy estimate (uniform in
\varepsilon
) of the perturbed VMB system (
3.2
) from the inequality (
4.2
) in Lemma
4.1
. We first analyze the unsigned functional
\mathscr{A}_{s}(G,E,B)
defined in (
3.190
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{s}(G,E,B)=\mathscr{A}_{s}(G)+\eta_{1}\mathscr{A}_{s}(E,B)\,,

where
\mathscr{A}_{s}(G)
is defined in (
3.36
) and
\mathscr{A}_{s}(E,B)
is given in (
3.129
). Via the Hölder inequality, one easily deduces that for
s\geq 3

(4.61)

\displaystyle|\mathscr{A}_{s}

\displaystyle(G)|\leq C\sum_{|m|\leq s-1}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg\{\|\partial^{m}_{x}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\big(\|\partial_{i}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial_{i}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\partial_{j}\partial^{m}_{x}u_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\zeta_{ij}(v)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\partial_{i}\partial^{m}_{x}\theta\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\widetilde{\zeta}_{i}(v)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}(\|\partial_{i}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial_{i}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}})(\|\zeta_{i}^{+}(v)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}+\|\zeta_{i}^{-}(v)\|_{L^{2}_{v}})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{|m|\leq s-1}\bigg\{\|\partial^{m}_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}(\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}})

\displaystyle+\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big(\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}\theta\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{|m|\leq s-1}\big(\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big)\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq C\!_{{}_{\#}}^{(1)}\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

for some
C\!_{{}_{\#}}^{(1)}>0
, independent of
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, where we make use of the facts

(4.62)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}\rho^{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}\theta\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq C\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,,

\displaystyle\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C\|\partial^{m}_{x}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
and
\zeta_{ij}(v)
,
\widetilde{\zeta}_{i}(v)
,
\zeta_{i}^{+}(v)
,
\zeta_{i}^{-}(v)\in L^{2}_{v}
. Considering the functional
\mathscr{A}_{s}(E,B)
, defined in (
3.129
), we yield that

(4.63)

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\big|\mathscr{A}_{s}

\displaystyle(E,B)\big|\leq C\sum_{|m|\leq s-1}\Big(\varepsilon\|\partial^{m}_{x}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\mathsf{q}_{1}\widetilde{\Phi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\partial^{m}_{x}E\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\sum_{|m|\leq s-2}\Big[\varepsilon\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\mathsf{q}_{1}\widetilde{\Phi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\mathsf{q}_{1}\widetilde{\Phi}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\big(\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}B\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}B\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{|m|\leq s-1}\big(\|\partial^{m}_{x}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}E\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\sum_{|m|\leq s-2}\Big[\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{x}G\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\big(\|\nabla_{x}\times\partial^{m}_{x}E\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}B\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\!_{{}_{\#}}^{(2)}\big(\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)

holds for any
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
and for some constant
C\!_{{}_{\#}}^{(2)}>0
, independent of
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. Here the Hölder inequality, the Young’s inequality, the bound
\|\mathsf{q}_{1}\widetilde{\Phi}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\leq C
and the third Faraday equation of (
3.2
) are utilized. Combining the bounds (
4.61
) and (
4.63
), one immediately yields that

(4.64)

\displaystyle\big|\mathscr{A}_{s}(G,E,B)\big|\leq

\displaystyle|\mathscr{A}_{s}(G)|+\eta_{1}|\mathscr{A}_{s}(E,B)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\!_{{}_{\#}}\big(\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)

for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
, where
C\!_{{}_{\#}}=\max\big\{C\!_{{}_{\#}}^{(1)},C\!_{{}_{\#}}^{(2)}\big\}>0
.

Now we take
\eta_{2}=\min\big\{\eta_{0},\tfrac{\varrho_{s}}{2\varrho_{s}^{*}C\!_{{}_{\#}}}\big\}>0
, where
\eta_{0}>0
is mentioned in Proposition
3.6
, the constants
\varrho_{s}>0
and
\varrho_{s}^{*}>0
are given in Lemma
4.1
. Then we introduce an energy functional

(4.65)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{s}(G,E,B)=\varrho_{s}\mathscr{E}_{\eta_{2}}(G,E,B)+\varrho_{s}^{*}\varepsilon\eta_{2}\mathscr{A}_{s}(G,E,B)+\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s,|\alpha|\leq s}\tfrac{C_{|\alpha|}\eta_{2}}{1+\eta_{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

and an energy dissipative rate

(4.66)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{s}(G,E,B)=\tfrac{\delta_{s}\eta_{2}}{1+\eta_{2}}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s,|\alpha|\leq s}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\delta_{s}^{*}\mathscr{D}_{\eta_{2}}(G,E,B)\,,

where the positive constants
\varrho_{s}
,
\varrho_{s}^{*}
,
C_{|\alpha|}

\delta_{s}
and
\delta_{s}^{*}
are mentioned in Lemma
4.1
. We remark that the chosen number
\eta_{2}=\min\big\{\eta_{0},\tfrac{\varrho_{s}}{2\varrho_{s}^{*}C\!_{{}_{\#}}}\big\}>0
is such that the energy
\mathcal{E}_{s}(G,E,B)
is nonnegative for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Indeed, by (
4.64
) we know that for any
0<\eta\leq\eta_{0}
and for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1

(4.67)

\displaystyle\varrho_{s}\mathscr{E}_{\eta}(G,E,B)+\varrho_{s}^{*}\varepsilon\eta\mathscr{A}_{s}(G,E,B)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varrho_{s}\big(\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)-\varrho_{s}^{*}\eta\big|\mathscr{A}_{s}(G,E,B)\big|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(\varrho_{s}-\varrho_{s}^{*}C\!_{{}_{\#}}\eta)\big(\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\,.

So, if we require the right-hand side of the previous inequality is nonnegative, we noly need to choose an
\eta>0
such that

\displaystyle\varrho_{s}-\varrho_{s}^{*}C\!_{{}_{\#}}\eta>0\qquad\textrm{and }\qquad 0<\eta\leq\eta_{0}\,.

Without loss of generality, we take

(4.68)

\displaystyle\eta=\eta_{2}=\min\big\{\eta_{0},\tfrac{\varrho_{s}}{2\varrho_{s}^{*}C\!_{{}_{\#}}}\big\}>0\,.

We thereby obtain the positivity of the energy
\mathcal{E}_{s}(G,E,B)
.

One notices that

(4.69)

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\leq C\mathcal{D}_{s}(G,E,B)

holds for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
and for some
C>0
, independent of
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. Therefore, from Lemma
4.1
, we immediately derive the following proposition.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
s\geq 3
be an integer and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Assume that
(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
is the solution to the perturbed VMB system (
1.9
) constructed in Proposition
3.1
. Then there is a constant
C>0
, independent of
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, such that

(4.70)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})+\mathcal{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\leq C\big[\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{2}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\big]\mathcal{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})

holds for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.

Based on the uniform inequality (
4.70
) in Proposition
4.1
, we will give the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
: Global solutions.

First, it is easy to know that there are constants
c_{0}
,
C_{0}>0
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that

(4.71)

\displaystyle c_{0}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\leq\mathcal{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\,,

\displaystyle c_{0}\mathbb{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\leq\mathcal{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\leq C_{0}\mathbb{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\,,

where the energy functional
\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
and energy dissipative rate functional
\mathbb{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
are defined in (
1.22
). From (
4.71
), the differential inequality (
4.70
) in Proposition
4.1
and the energy bound (
3.1
) in Proposition
3.1
, we deduce that for any
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subset[0,T^{*}]
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1

(4.72)

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(t_{2})-\mathcal{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(t_{1})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\big[\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{2}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\big]\mathcal{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})+\mathbb{E}_{s}^{2}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\Big]\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathbb{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\sqrt{\ell}+\ell^{2})\left[\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\mathrm{d}t+\ell(t_{2}-t_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0\quad\textrm{as}\quad t_{2}\rightarrow t_{1}\,,

where
\ell>0
is mentioned in Proposition
3.1
. Thus the local solution
(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
constructed in Proposition
3.1
is such that the energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(t)
is continuous in
t\in[0,T^{*}]
.

We now define

(4.73)

\displaystyle T=\sup\Big\{\tau\geq 0;C\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\big[\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{2}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\big](t)\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\Big\}\geq 0\,.

From the relations (
4.71
) and the initial condition in Theorem
1.1
, we have

(4.74)

\displaystyle c_{0}\mathbb{E}(G_{\varepsilon}^{in},E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})\leq\mathcal{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(0)\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}(G_{\varepsilon}^{in},E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})\leq C_{0}\ell_{0}\,,

where
\ell_{0}\in(0,1]
is small to be determined. If we take
0<\ell_{0}\leq\min\big\{1,\ell,\tfrac{1}{16C^{2}(\sqrt{C_{0}}+C_{0}^{2})^{2}}\big\}
(
\ell>0
is mentioned in Proposition
3.1
), we have

(4.75)

\displaystyle C\big[\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(0)+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{2}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(0)\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\sqrt{C_{0}\ell_{0}}+(C_{0}\ell_{0})^{2}\big)\leq C(\sqrt{C_{0}}+C_{0}^{2})\sqrt{\ell_{0}}\leq\tfrac{1}{4}<\tfrac{1}{2}\,.

Then, the continuity of
\mathcal{E}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(t)
implies that
T>0
. Consequently, we derive from the definition of
T
and the inequality (
4.70
) that for all
t\in[0,T]
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1

(4.76)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\leq 0\,.

Then we yield that for all
t\in[0,T]
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1

(4.77)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\mathcal{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(0)\leq C_{0}\ell_{0}\,,

which immediately implies by the initial bound (
4.75
) that

(4.78)

\displaystyle C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big[\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{2}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})\big](t)\leq\tfrac{1}{4}<\tfrac{1}{2}\,.

Thus, the continuity of
\mathcal{E}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(t)
implies that
T=+\infty
. In other words, the local solution constructed in Proposition
3.1
can be extended globally. Moreover, the uniform energy bound (
1.24
) can be derived from (
4.71
) and (
4.77
). Then the proof of Theorem
1.1
is completed. ∎

5.
Limit to two fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell equations with Ohm’s law

In this section, we will derive the two fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell equations (
1.25
) with Ohm’s law from the perturbed two-species Vlasov-Maxwell-Boltzmann (
1.9
) as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

5.1.
Local conservation laws

We first introduce the following fluid variables

(5.1)

\displaystyle\rho_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,\ u_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,\ \theta_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}=\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,\ j_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,\ w_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,.

Then we can derive the following local conservation laws from the solutions
(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
constructed in Theorem
1.1
. These conservation laws exactly can be referred to
[
5
]
. However, for convenience for readers, we justify them here.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume that
(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
is the solutions to the perturbed two-species VMB equations (
1.9
) constructed in Theorem
1.1
. Then the following local conservation laws holds:

(5.2)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\rho_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}=0\,,\\
\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{1}{2}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}+j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\,,\\
\partial_{t}\theta_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{2}{3}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}\,,\\
\partial_{t}n_{\varepsilon}+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j_{\varepsilon}=0\,,\\
\partial_{t}E_{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B_{\varepsilon}=-j_{\varepsilon}\,,\\
\partial_{t}B_{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E_{\varepsilon}=0\,,\\
\mathrm{div}_{x}\,E_{\varepsilon}=n_{\varepsilon}\,,\quad\mathrm{div}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}=0\,.\end{array}\right.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

From the definition of
j_{\varepsilon}
in (
5.1
), we easily derive last four relations of (
5.2
). We thereby need only verify the first four relations in (
5.2
).

Step 1. Conservation law of
\rho_{\varepsilon}
.
We multiply the first
G_{\varepsilon}
-equation of (
1.9
) by
\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}}{2}\in\textrm{Ker}(\mathscr{L})
and integrate over
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then we obtain

(5.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{\varepsilon}+\underset{I_{1}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle v\cdot\nabla_{x}G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}+\underset{I_{2}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{I_{3}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\big\langle\mathscr{L}G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}\ \ \underset{I_{4}}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)\sqrt{M}\mathsf{q}_{1},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}

\displaystyle=\underset{I_{5}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}+\underset{I_{6}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon},G_{\varepsilon}),\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}\,.

For the term
I_{1}
, we have

(5.4)

\displaystyle I_{1}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle v\sqrt{M},\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}\,.

For the term
I_{2}
, we compute that

(5.5)

\displaystyle I_{2}=-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2}\nabla_{v}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{1}{2}E_{\varepsilon}\cdot\big\langle\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2},v\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,.

Since
\mathsf{q}_{1}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}=0
, we know that
I_{4}=0
. For the term
I_{5}
, we have

(5.6)

\displaystyle I_{5}=\tfrac{1}{2}E_{\varepsilon}\cdot\big\langle\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2},v\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,.

Collecting the above relations, we deduce that

(5.7)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}=0\,,

hence the first equation of (
5.2
) holds.

Step 2. Conservation law of
u_{\varepsilon}
.
Multiplying the first
G_{\varepsilon}
-equation of (
1.9
) by
\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}}{2}\in\textrm{Ker}(\mathscr{L})
and integrating over
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

(5.8)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}+\underset{I\!I_{1}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle v\cdot\nabla_{x}G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}+\underset{I\!I_{2}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{I\!I_{3}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\big\langle\mathscr{L}G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}\ \ \underset{I\!I_{4}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)\sqrt{M}\mathsf{q}_{1},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}

\displaystyle=\underset{I\!I_{5}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}+\underset{I\!I_{6}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon},G_{\varepsilon}),\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}\,,

where
I\!I_{4}=0
is derived from
\mathsf{q}_{1}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}=0
. For the term
I\!I_{1}
, we deduce that

(5.9)

\displaystyle I\!I_{1}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle v\otimes v\sqrt{M},\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle A(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3}\sqrt{M},\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\Big(\big\langle G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\big\langle G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}}{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\,,

where
\mathcal{L}
is the standard linearized Boltzmann collision operator,
A(v)=v\otimes v-\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3}
with
A\sqrt{M}\in\textrm{Ker}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L})
,
\widehat{A}(v)
is such that
\mathcal{L}(\widehat{A}\sqrt{M})=A\sqrt{M}
with
\widehat{A}\sqrt{M}\in\textrm{Ker}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L})
and
\mathbb{I}_{3}
is the
3\times 3
unitary matrix. For the term
I\!I_{2}
, we have

(5.10)

\displaystyle I\!I_{2}=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle G_{\varepsilon},(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{1}v\sqrt{M}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle G_{\varepsilon},(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot(\mathbb{I}_{3}-\tfrac{1}{2}v\otimes v)\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}}{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}E_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{1}{2\varepsilon}\big\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}v\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\times B_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{4}\big\langle E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v,G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{1}{2}j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{4}\big\langle E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v,G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,

where the cancellation
(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot v=0
is utilized. For the term
I\!I_{5}
, we derive that

(5.11)

\displaystyle I\!I_{5}=\tfrac{1}{4}\big\langle E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v,G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,.

Collecting the previous calculations of the terms
I\!I_{1}
,
I\!I_{2}
and
I\!I_{5}
, we obtain

(5.12)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{1}{2}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}+j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\,,

then the second equations of (
5.2
) holds.

Step 3. Conservation law of
\theta_{\varepsilon}
.
We take
L^{2}_{v}
-inner product via multiplying the first
G_{\varepsilon}
-equation of (
1.9
) by
\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\in\textrm{Ker}(\mathscr{L})
. More precisely, we have

(5.13)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta_{\varepsilon}+\underset{I\!I\!I_{1}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle v\cdot\nabla_{x}G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{I\!I\!I_{2}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{I\!I\!I_{3}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\big\langle\mathscr{L}G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}\ \ \underset{I\!I\!I_{4}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}

\displaystyle=\underset{I\!I\!I_{5}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}+\underset{I\!I\!I_{6}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon},G_{\varepsilon}),\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}\,,

where
I\!I\!I_{3}=0
is implied by
\mathsf{q}_{1}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}=0
. For the term
I\!I\!I_{1}
, we have

(5.14)

\displaystyle I\!I\!I_{1}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M},v(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{3\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M},v(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{5}{2})+v\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{3}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\big\langle\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2},B(v)\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{3}\mathrm{div}_{x}\big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})+\tfrac{2}{3}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}\,,

where
B(v)=v(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{5}{2})
with
B\sqrt{M}\in\textrm{Ker}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L})
and
\widehat{B}(v)
is such that
\mathcal{L}(\widehat{B}\sqrt{M})=B\sqrt{M}
with
\widehat{B}(v)\sqrt{M}\in\textrm{Ker}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L})
. For the term
I\!I\!I_{2}
, we have

(5.15)

\displaystyle I\!I\!I_{2}=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2\varepsilon}\big\langle G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}[(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}]\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},E_{\varepsilon}\cdot[\tfrac{2}{3}v-\tfrac{1}{2}v(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)]\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3}E_{\varepsilon}\cdot\big\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}v\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tfrac{1}{4}\big\langle E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v,G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}+I\!I\!I_{5}\,,

where we make use of the cancellation
(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot v=0
. Thus, collecting the all relations derived in the previous yields that

(5.16)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}\,,

and the third equation of (
5.2
) holds.

Conservation law of
n_{\varepsilon}
.
We take
L^{2}_{v}
-inner product via multiplying the first equation of (
1.9
) by
\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\in\textrm{Ker}(\mathscr{L})
. We then obtain

(5.17)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}n_{\varepsilon}+\underset{I\!V_{1}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle v\cdot\nabla_{x}G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}+\underset{I\!V_{2}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{I\!V_{3}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\big\langle\mathscr{L}G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}\ \ \underset{I\!V_{4}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)\sqrt{M}\mathsf{q}_{1},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}

\displaystyle=\underset{I\!V_{5}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}+\underset{I\!V_{6}\ =\,0}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon},G_{\varepsilon}),\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}}}\,,

where
I\!V_{4}=0
is derived from
\langle v\sqrt{M},\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=0
. For the term
I\!V_{1}
, we have

(5.18)

\displaystyle I\!V_{1}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j_{\varepsilon}\,.

For the term
I\!V_{2}
, we deduce from the integration by parts over
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and the cancellation
(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot v=0
that

(5.19)

\displaystyle I\!V_{2}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}),\mathsf{q}_{1}\tfrac{1}{2}v\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v,G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=I\!V_{5}\,.

Then, we derive the fourth equation of (
5.2
)

(5.20)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}n_{\varepsilon}+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j_{\varepsilon}=0

from the microscopic kinetic
G_{\varepsilon}
-equation of (
1.9
). We emphasize that the conservation law of
n_{\varepsilon}
can also be derived from the Amperé equation
\partial_{t}E_{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B_{\varepsilon}=-j_{\varepsilon}
with the constraint
\mathrm{div}_{x}\,E_{\varepsilon}=n_{\varepsilon}
, which means that the conservation law of
n_{\varepsilon}
demonstrates the unity of micro and macro. As a result, the proof of Lemma
5.1
is finished.
∎

5.2.
Limits from the global energy estimate

Based on Theorem
1.1
, the Cauchy problem (
1.9
)-(
1.21
) admits a global solution
(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
belonging to
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v})
,
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})
and
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})
, which subjects to the global energy estimate (
1.24
), namely, there is a positive constant, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that

(5.21)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\big(\|G_{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}+\|E_{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\leq C\,,

and

(5.22)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\mathrm{d}t\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\,.

From the energy bound (
5.21
), there are
G\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v})
,
E,B\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})
, such that

(5.23)

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow G\quad\textrm{weakly-}\star\ \textrm{for}\ t\geq 0\,,\ \textrm{weakly in }H^{s}_{x,v}\,,

\displaystyle E_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow E\quad\textrm{weakly-}\star\ \textrm{for}\ t\geq 0\,,\ \textrm{weakly in }H^{s}_{x}\,,

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow B\quad\textrm{weakly-}\star\ \textrm{for}\ t\geq 0\,,\ \textrm{weakly in }H^{s}_{x}\,,

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. The limits may hold for some subsequences. But, for convenience, we still employ the original notations of the sequences to denote by the subsequences throughout this paper. From the energy dissipation bound (
5.22
) and the inequality
\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\leq C\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}
derived from the part (1) of Lemma
2.2
, we have

(5.24)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0\quad\textrm{strongly in }\ L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v})

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. We thereby deduce from combining the first convergence in (
5.23
) and (
5.24
) that

(5.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G=0\,,

which immediately means that there are
(\rho^{+},\rho^{-},u,\theta)\in L^{\infty}_{t}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})
such that

(5.26)

\displaystyle G(t,x,v)=

\displaystyle\rho^{+}(t,x)\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{1}+\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\sqrt{M(v)}+\rho^{-}(t,x)\tfrac{\mathsf{q}_{2}-\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2}\sqrt{M(v)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle u(t,x)\cdot v\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M(v)}+\theta(t,x)(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M(v)}\,,

where
\mathsf{q}_{1}=[1,-1]\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\mathsf{q}_{2}=[1,1]\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Via the definitions of
\rho_{\varepsilon}
,
u_{\varepsilon}
,
\theta_{\varepsilon}
and
n_{\varepsilon}
in (
5.1
) and the uniform energy bound (
5.21
), we obtain

(5.27)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\big(\|\rho_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|n_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\leq C\,.

We thereby deduce the following convergences from the convergence of (
5.23
) and the limit function
G(t,x,v)
given in (
5.26
) that

(5.28)

\displaystyle\rho_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\overset{\triangle}{=}\rho\,,

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=u\,,

\displaystyle\theta_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G,\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\theta\,,

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}=\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow\langle G,\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\rho^{+}-\rho^{-}\overset{\triangle}{=}n\,,

weakly-
\star
for
t\geq 0
and weakly in
H^{s}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. It remains to find the limits of
j_{\varepsilon}
and
w_{\varepsilon}
defined in (
5.1
). We first analyze the relations between
\Pi^{\perp}_{\mathcal{L}}G_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}
. By the definition of the operator
\Pi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\perp}
, we have

(5.29)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{+}=

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}^{+}-\sum_{1\leq i\leq 5}\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{+},\chi_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\chi_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}^{+}-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},(\mathsf{q}_{1}+\mathsf{q}_{2})\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\sqrt{M}

\displaystyle-\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big]\cdot v\sqrt{M}

\displaystyle-\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{2}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\langle G_{\varepsilon},\mathsf{q}_{1}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big](\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}^{+}-(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon})^{+}-\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot v\sqrt{M}-\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}w_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})^{+}-\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot v\sqrt{M}-\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}w_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}\,,

where the relations (
1.18
) and (
1.16
) are utilized. Moreover, similar calculations in the previous process (
5.29
) yield that

(5.30)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{-}=(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})^{+}+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot v\sqrt{M}+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}w_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}\,.

We thereby derive from (
5.29
) and (
5.30
) that

(5.31)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{m}_{x}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})^{\pm}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=

\displaystyle\|\partial^{m}_{x}\Pi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{4}\|\partial^{m}_{x}j_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\langle v\sqrt{M},v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{4}\|\partial^{m}_{x}w_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\langle(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M},(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\partial^{m}_{x}\Pi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{4}\|\partial^{m}_{x}j_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\tfrac{3\varepsilon^{2}}{8}\|\partial^{m}_{x}w_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}

for all
|m|\leq s
, where
\langle v\sqrt{M},v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=1
and
\langle(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M},(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{3}{2}
are used. Then, from the part (1) of Lemma
2.2
, the energy dissipation bound (
5.22
) and the relation (
5.31
), we deduce that

(5.32)

\displaystyle\|j_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}+\|w_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\mathrm{d}t\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\mathrm{d}t\leq C^{*}\,.

Consequently, there are functions
j
,
w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})
such that

(5.33)

\displaystyle j_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow j\quad\textrm{and }\ w_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow w

weakly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

5.3.
Convergences to limiting equations

In this subsection, we will derive the two fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell equations (
1.25
) with Ohm’s law from the conservation laws (
5.2
) in Lemma
5.1
and the convergences obtained in the previous subsection.

5.3.1.
Incompressibility and Boussinesq relation

From the first equation of (
5.2
) in Lemma
5.1
and the energy uniform bound (
5.21
), it is easy to deduce

(5.34)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}\rho_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distributions as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, which imply that by combining with the convergence (
5.28
)

(5.35)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u=0\,.

Via the second equation of (
5.2
), we have

(5.36)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}-\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}+j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\,.

Noticing that

(5.37)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}+\rho_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{M}+u_{\varepsilon}\cdot v\sqrt{M}+\theta_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}\,,

one has

(5.38)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})=\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\,.

Thus, we have

(5.39)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle A(v)\sqrt{M},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,

where the self-adjointness of
\mathcal{L}
is utilized. Then we derive from the Hölder inequality, the part (1) of Lemma
2.2
and the uniform energy dissipation bound (
5.22
) that

(5.40)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big\|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\mathrm{d}t\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\mathrm{d}t\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\,.

Furthermore, from the uniform bounds (
5.21
), (
5.27
) and (
5.32
), we easily derive that

(5.41)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\big\|\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}+j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\big\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\leq C\varepsilon\,.

Consequently, it is easy to deduce that

(5.42)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distributions as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, which, combining with the convergence (
5.28
), gives the Boussinesq relation

(5.43)

\displaystyle\rho+\theta=0\,.

5.3.2.
Convergences of
\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon}
,
\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}
,
n_{\varepsilon}
,
E_{\varepsilon}
and
B_{\varepsilon}

Before doing this, we introduce the following Aubin-Lions-Simon Theorem, a fundamental result of compactness in the study of nonlinear evolution problems, which can be referred to Theorem II.5.16 of
[
11
]
or
[
58
]
, for instance.

Lemma 5.2

(Aubin-Lions-Simon Theorem)
.

Let
B_{0}\subset B_{1}\subset B_{2}
be three Banach spaces. We assume that the embedding of
B_{1}
in
B_{2}
is continuous ans that the embedding of
B_{0}
in
B_{1}
is compact. Let
p
,
r
be such that
1\leq p,r\leq+\infty
. For
T>0
, we define

(5.44)

\displaystyle E_{p,r}=\Big\{u\in L^{p}(0,T;B_{0}),\partial_{t}u\in L^{r}(0,T;B_{2})\Big\}\,.

(1)

If
p<+\infty
, the embedding of
E_{p,r}
in
L^{p}(0,T;B_{1})
is compact.

(2)

If
p=+\infty
and if
r>1
, the embedding of
E_{p,r}
in
C(0,T;B_{1})
.

We emphasize that the reflexivity of the spaces considered in Lemma
5.2
is not assumed.

We now consider the convergence of
\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon}
. The third equation of (
5.2
) multiplied by
\tfrac{3}{5}
minus
\tfrac{2}{5}
times of the first equation of (
5.2
) gives

(5.45)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})+\tfrac{2}{5}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{\varepsilon}{5}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}\,.

Noticing the relation (
5.38
), we yield that

(5.46)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}=

\displaystyle\Big\|\tfrac{\varepsilon}{5}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\|\tfrac{\varepsilon}{5}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\|\tfrac{\varepsilon}{5}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{1}{5\varepsilon}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle B(v)\sqrt{M},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|B(v)\sqrt{M}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|j_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,

which immediately derives from the uniform energy bounds (
5.21
), (
5.27
) and (
5.32
) that

(5.47)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s-1}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|j_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu))}\leq C

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. It is easily derived from the definition of
\rho_{\varepsilon}
,
\theta_{\varepsilon}
in (
5.1
) and the uniform energy bound (
5.21
) that

(5.48)

\displaystyle\big\|(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})\big\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s}_{x})}\leq C

for all
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. One notices that

(5.49)

\displaystyle H^{s}_{x}\hookrightarrow H^{s-1}_{x}\hookrightarrow H^{s-1}_{x}\,,

where the embedding of
H^{s}_{x}
in
H^{s-1}_{x}
is compact and the embedding of
H^{s-1}_{x}
in
H^{s-1}_{x}
is naturally continuous. Then, from Aubin-Lions-Simon Theorem in Lemma
5.2
, the bounds (
5.47
), (
5.48
) and the embeddings (
5.49
), we deduce that there is a
\widetilde{\theta}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
such that

(5.50)

\displaystyle\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\widetilde{\theta}

strongly in
C(0,T;H^{s-1}_{x})
for any
T>0
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Combining with the convergences (
5.28
), we know that
\widetilde{\theta}=\tfrac{3}{5}\theta-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho
. Then the relation (
5.43
) and
\theta=(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho)+\tfrac{2}{5}(\rho+\theta)
give us
\widetilde{\theta}=\theta
. As a result,

(5.51)

\displaystyle\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\theta

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
. Noticing that
\theta_{\varepsilon}=(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})+\tfrac{2}{5}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})
, we thereby derive from the convergences (
5.28
) that

(5.52)

\displaystyle\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0

weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
, weakly in
H^{s}_{x}
and strongly in
H^{s-1}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

Next we consider the convergence of
\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}
, where
\mathcal{P}
is the Leray projection operator. Taking
\mathcal{P}
on the second equation of (
5.2
) gives

(5.53)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}+j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\,.

It is easy to derive from the relation (
5.38
), the Hölder inequality, the bound
\|A(v)\sqrt{M}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\leq C
, the calculus inequality and the part (1) of Lemma
2.2
that

(5.54)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}=

\displaystyle\Big\|\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}+j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\|\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}+j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\|\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}+j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big\langle A(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{2\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+C\|j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|A(v)\sqrt{M}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|n_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+C\|j_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|B_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,

which, by the uniform energy bounds (
5.21
), (
5.27
) and (
5.32
), implies that

(5.55)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s-1}_{x})}\leq C\|n_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})}\sqrt{T}

\displaystyle+C\|j_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\|B_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sqrt{T}+C

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Furthermore, from the definition of
u_{\varepsilon}
in (
5.1
) and the uniform energy bound (
5.21
), we derive that for all
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1

(5.56)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s}_{x})}\leq C\,.

Then, from Aubin-Lions-Simon Theorem in Lemma
5.2
, the bounds (
5.55
), (
5.56
) and the embeddings (
5.49
), we derive that there is a
\widetilde{u}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
such that

(5.57)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\widetilde{u}

strongly in
C(0,T;H^{s-1}_{x})
for all
T>0
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Furthermore, from the convergences (
5.28
) and the incompressibility (
5.35
), we deduce

(5.58)

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}=\mathcal{P}u=u\,.

Consequently,

(5.59)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow u

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
. Furthermore, we know that

(5.60)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0

weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
, weakly in
H^{s}_{x}
and strongly in
H^{s-1}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

Next, we consider the convergence of
n_{\varepsilon}
. From the local conservation laws (
5.2
) in Lemma
5.1
, we know that
n_{\varepsilon}
satisfies

(5.61)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}n_{\varepsilon}+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j_{\varepsilon}=0\,.

Then, we have

(5.62)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}n_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}=\|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq C\|j_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\,,

which yields by using the bound (
5.32
) that

(5.63)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}n_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s-1}_{x})}\leq C\|j_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\leq C

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Moreover, from the bound (
5.27
), we know that for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1

(5.64)

\displaystyle\|n_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s}_{x})}\leq C\,.

Then, it is derived from Aubin-Lions-Simon Theorem in Lemma
5.2
, the bounds (
5.63
), (
5.64
) and the embeddings (
5.49
) that
n_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow n
strongly in
C(0,T;H^{s-1}_{x})
for any
T>0
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Hence, we have

(5.65)

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow n

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
n\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
.

We next consider the convergences of
E_{\varepsilon}
and
B_{\varepsilon}
. Noticing that
E_{\varepsilon}
and
B_{\varepsilon}
subject to

(5.66)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}E_{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B_{\varepsilon}=-j_{\varepsilon}\,,\ \partial_{t}B_{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E_{\varepsilon}=0\,,

we deduce that

(5.67)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}E_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}B_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\times B_{\varepsilon}-j_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}\times E_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|B_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|j_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x}})\,,

which reduces to

(5.68)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}E_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s-1}_{x})}+\|\partial_{t}B_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s-1}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|B_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}+\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})})\sqrt{T}+C\|j_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\sqrt{T}+1)

for all
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Here the uniform energy bounds (
5.21
) and (
5.32
) are utilized. Moreover, from the bound (
5.21
), we have

(5.69)

\displaystyle\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s}_{x})}+\|B_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s}_{x})}\leq C

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Then, from Aubin-Lions-Simon Theorem in Lemma
5.2
, the uniform bounds (
5.68
), (
5.69
) and the embeddings (
5.49
), we deduce that
E_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow E
and
B_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow B
strongly in
C(0,T;H^{s-1}_{x})
for any
T>0
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Namely, we have

(5.70)

\displaystyle E_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow E\quad\textrm{and}\quad B_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow B

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
E,B\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
.

In summary, we have deduced the following convergences:

(5.71)

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon},\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon},n_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon}\big)\rightarrow(u,\theta,n,E,B)

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
(u,\theta,n,E,B)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
, and

(5.72)

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon},\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}\big)\rightarrow(0,0)

weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
, weakly in
H^{s}_{x}
and strongly in
H^{s-1}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, and

(5.73)

\displaystyle(j_{\varepsilon},w_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow(j,w)

weakly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
(j,w)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})
.

5.3.3.
Ohm’s law and energy equivalence relation

We now derive the Ohm’s law in the last second equality of (
1.25
) and energy equivalence relation in the last first equality of (
1.25
). As in (
3.115
), multiplying the first
G_{\varepsilon}
-equation of (
1.9
) by
\mathsf{q}_{1}
gives

(5.74)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{L}+\mathfrak{L})(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})-v\cdot\nabla_{x}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})-(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})

\displaystyle+2E_{\varepsilon}\cdot\Phi(v)+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})+\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon},G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}\,,

where
\Phi(v)=v\sqrt{M}
and the operator
\mathfrak{L}
is defined in (
3.114
). By the definition of
\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}
in (
1.16
),
\mathsf{q}_{1}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}=2
and
\mathsf{q}_{1}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}=0
, we have

(5.75)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}=n_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{M}

and

(5.76)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}=\rho_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{M}+\underset{2h_{\varepsilon}}{\underbrace{2u_{\varepsilon}\cdot v\sqrt{M}+2\theta_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}}}\,.

Then, by the decomposition
G_{\varepsilon}=\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}+\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}
, the relations (
5.75
) and (
5.76
), we can calculate

(5.77)

\displaystyle v\cdot\nabla_{x}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})=

\displaystyle v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}n_{\varepsilon}\cdot\Phi(v)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})\,,

and

(5.78)

\displaystyle(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})=

\displaystyle(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})+(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot v\big(\rho_{\varepsilon}+2u_{\varepsilon}\cdot v+2\theta_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\big)\sqrt{M}

\displaystyle+(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot u_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{M}+(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\Phi(v)+(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})\,,

where the cancellation
(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot v=0
and the relation
(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot u_{\varepsilon}=-(u_{\varepsilon}\cdot B_{\varepsilon})\cdot v
are utilized. Furthermore, we have

(5.79)

\displaystyle\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon},G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}=\Gamma(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}+\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}

\displaystyle+\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}+\Gamma(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}\,.

For the term
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}
, we derive from the fact
\mathcal{Q}(\sqrt{M},\sqrt{M})=0
, the definition of
h_{\varepsilon}\in\textrm{Ker}(\mathcal{L})
in (
5.76
), the cancellation
\mathcal{L}h_{\varepsilon}=0
, the relations (
5.75
)-(
5.76
), the definitions of operators
\mathcal{L}
in (
1.11
) and
\mathfrak{L}
in (
3.114
) that

(5.80)

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{+},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{+}+\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{-})-\mathcal{Q}(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{-},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{+}+\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{+}-\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{-},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{+}+\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(n_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{M},\rho_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{M}+2h_{\varepsilon})=2n_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{Q}(\sqrt{M},h_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}\big[\mathcal{Q}(h_{\varepsilon},\sqrt{M})+\mathcal{Q}(\sqrt{M},h_{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{Q}(h_{\varepsilon},\sqrt{M})+\mathcal{Q}(\sqrt{M},h_{\varepsilon})\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-n_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}h_{\varepsilon}+n_{\varepsilon}\mathfrak{L}h_{\varepsilon}=n_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{L}+\mathfrak{L})h_{\varepsilon}-2n_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}h_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{L}+\mathfrak{L})h_{\varepsilon}\,.

Consequently, from the previous equalities and the relations (
5.75
)-(
5.76
), we have

(5.81)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{L}+\mathfrak{L})(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})=2\big(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n_{\varepsilon}+E_{\varepsilon}+u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\Phi(v)+n_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{L}+\mathfrak{L})h_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}\,,

where
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}
has the form of

(5.82)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\partial_{t}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})-v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})-\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})

\displaystyle-(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})

\displaystyle+\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}+\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}+\Gamma(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}\,.

As shown in the equality (
3.123
), via multiplying the relation (
5.81
) by
\widetilde{\Phi}(v)
defined in (
3.121
), using Lemma
3.1
and the definition of
j_{\varepsilon}
in (
5.1
), we obtain

(5.83)

\displaystyle j_{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle 2(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n_{\varepsilon}+E_{\varepsilon}+u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\langle\Phi(v)\otimes\widehat{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\langle n_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon},\Phi(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\langle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n_{\varepsilon}+E_{\varepsilon}+u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\tfrac{1}{2}\sigma\mathbb{I}_{3}+n_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon}+\langle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon}+\sigma(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n_{\varepsilon}+E_{\varepsilon}+u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})+\langle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,.

Then, we have

(5.84)

\displaystyle j_{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}+\sigma(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n_{\varepsilon}+E_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle+n_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}+\sigma\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon}+\langle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

by utilizing the decomposition
u_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}
, where
\mathcal{P}
is the Leray projection. From the convergences (
5.71
), we easily know that for
s\geq 3

(5.85)

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}+\sigma(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n_{\varepsilon}+E_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow nu+\sigma(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n+E+u\times B)

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-2}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Moreover, it is easily deduced from the convergences (
5.71
) and (
5.72
) that

(5.86)

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}+\sigma\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0

weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
and weakly in
H^{s-1}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

Next we prove that

(5.87)

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distribution as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Indeed, for any
T>0
, let a vector-valued text function
\psi(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3}))
,
\psi(0,x)=\psi_{0}(x)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
and
\psi(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
. Then, from the uniform bound (
5.21
) and the initial energy bounds given in Theorem
1.1
that

(5.88)

\displaystyle\Big|-\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{t}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\psi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{in}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\psi_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon\|\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\big(\|G_{\varepsilon}^{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};L^{2}_{x,v})}\|\partial_{t}\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}_{x})}T\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\psi,T)\varepsilon\big(\|G_{\varepsilon}^{in}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}+\|G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v})}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{*}(\psi,T)\varepsilon\rightarrow 0

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, which means that

(5.89)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\langle\partial_{t}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distribution as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. It is yielded that by the Hölder inequality and the part (1) of Lemma
2.2

(5.90)

\displaystyle\big\|-\langle v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|v\otimes\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\leq C\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,

which implies by the uniform energy dissipation bound (
5.22
) that

(5.91)

\displaystyle\big\|-\langle v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})}\leq C\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu))}\leq C\varepsilon\,.

Then we have

(5.92)

\displaystyle-\langle v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow 0

strongly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Similarly, we can estimate by using the bounds (
5.21
) and (
5.22
) that

(5.93)

\displaystyle\|-\langle(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|B_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu))}\leq C\varepsilon\,.

Therefore, we have

(5.94)

\displaystyle-\langle(v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow 0

strongly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. By the Hölder inequality and the energy bound (
5.21
), we have

(5.95)

\displaystyle\big\|\langle\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})-\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon\|v\otimes\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\|E_{\varepsilon}\otimes G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v})}

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon\|\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\|E_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\big(\|G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v})}+\|\nabla_{v}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v})}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\|G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v})}\leq C\varepsilon\,.

We thereby obtain

(5.96)

\displaystyle\langle\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})-\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow 0

strongly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
