\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. For any
T>0
, we take any vector-valued test function
\varphi(t,x)\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3})
. Then, by employing the uniform bound (
5.22
), Lemma
2.2
and
2.4
, we yield that

(5.97)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\varphi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\langle\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}),\mathsf{q}_{1}\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\varphi(t,x)\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|\mathsf{q}_{1}\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\varphi(t,x)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varphi)\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu))}\leq C(\varphi)\varepsilon^{2}\,.

Thus, we know that

(5.98)

\displaystyle\langle\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distribution as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Analogously, one easily derives that

(5.99)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\Gamma(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}+\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\varphi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(\varphi)\sqrt{T}\|G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v})}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu))}\leq C(\varphi,T)\varepsilon\,,

which immediately implies that

(5.100)

\displaystyle\langle\Gamma(\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}+\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon})\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distribution as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Combining the convergences (
5.89
), (
5.92
), (
5.94
), (
5.96
), (
5.98
), (
5.100
) with the definition of
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}
in (
5.82
), we deduce the validity of the convergence (
5.87
). Consequently, based on the convergences (
5.73
), (
5.85
), (
5.86
) and (
5.87
), the equality (
5.84
) implies that

(5.101)

\displaystyle j=nu+\sigma\big(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n+E+u\times B\big)\,.

Next we verify rigorously the energy equivalence relation, hence the last equation in (
1.25
). Recall that
\Psi(v)=(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}
and
\widetilde{\Psi}(v)
is such that
(\mathcal{L}+\mathfrak{L})\widetilde{\Psi}(v)=\Psi(v)
, which can be seen in Lemma
3.1
. Then, from the definition of
w_{\varepsilon}
in (
5.1
) and the equation (
5.81
), we deduce that

(5.102)

\displaystyle w_{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1},\Psi(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\big\langle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{L}+\mathfrak{L})(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n_{\varepsilon}+E_{\varepsilon}+u_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\langle\Phi(v),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\langle(\mathcal{L}+\mathfrak{L})(n_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\langle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle n_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon},\Psi(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\langle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,

where
\langle\Phi(v),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=0
and the self-adjointness of
\mathcal{L}+\mathfrak{L}
are also utilized. Moreover, direct calculation reduces to

(5.103)

\displaystyle\langle n_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon},\Psi(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=

\displaystyle n_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\langle v\sqrt{M},(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+n_{\varepsilon}\theta_{\varepsilon}\langle(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M},(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2})\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{3}{2}n_{\varepsilon}\theta_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{3}{2}n_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})+\tfrac{3}{5}n_{\varepsilon}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\,.

We thereby have

(5.104)

\displaystyle w_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{3}{2}n_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})+\tfrac{3}{5}n_{\varepsilon}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})+\langle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,.

From the strong convergences (
5.71
), one easily deduces that

(5.105)

\displaystyle\tfrac{3}{2}n_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow\tfrac{3}{2}n\theta

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. It is also derived from the convergences (
5.71
) and (
5.72
) that

(5.106)

\displaystyle\tfrac{3}{5}n_{\varepsilon}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow 0

weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
and weakly in
H^{s-1}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Furthermore, similar arguments in analyzing the convergence (
5.87
) give

(5.107)

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distribution as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Finally, by plugging the convergences (
5.73
), (
5.105
), (
5.106
) and (
5.107
) into the equation (
5.104
), we obtain the energy equivalence relation

(5.108)

\displaystyle w=\tfrac{3}{2}n\theta\,.

5.3.4.
Equations of
u
and
\theta

We first calculate the term

\big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,,

where
\widehat{\Xi}=A
or
B
. Via multiplying the first
G_{\varepsilon}
-equation in (
1.9
) by
\mathsf{q}_{2}
and direct calculations, we obtain

(5.109)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathcal{L}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1})+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{Q}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2},G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})\,.

Following the standard formal derivations of fluid dynamic limits of Boltzmann equation (see
[
6
]
, for instance), we obtain

(5.110)

\displaystyle\big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=u_{\varepsilon}\otimes u_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{|u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3}-\mu\Sigma(u_{\varepsilon})-R_{\varepsilon,A}

and

(5.111)

\displaystyle\big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\tfrac{5}{2}u_{\varepsilon}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{5}{2}\kappa\nabla_{x}\theta_{\varepsilon}-R_{\varepsilon,B}\,,

where
\Sigma(u_{\varepsilon})=\nabla_{x}u_{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}u_{\varepsilon}^{\top}-\tfrac{2}{3}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{I}_{3}
, and for
\Xi=A
or
B
,
R_{\varepsilon,\Xi}
are of the form

(5.112)

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle{}_{\varepsilon,\Xi}=\varepsilon\big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{M},\partial_{t}(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{M}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{M},(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}(\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{M},\tfrac{1}{2}(E_{\varepsilon}\cdot v)(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}}{2})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}-2\big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{M},\mathcal{Q}\big(\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2},\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle-2\big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{M},\mathcal{Q}\big(\Pi(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}),\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2}\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}-2\big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{M},\mathcal{Q}\big(\tfrac{\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2},\Pi(\tfrac{G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}}{2})\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\,.

Here we also make use of the relation
\Pi^{\perp}(G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2})=\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{2}
, derived from the equalities (
5.29
) and (
5.30
).

For the vector field
u_{\varepsilon}
, we decompose
u_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}
, where
\mathcal{P}^{\perp}=\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{P}=\nabla_{x}\Delta_{x}^{-1}\mathrm{div}_{x}
is a gradient operator, where
\mathcal{I}
is the identical mapping. Then, via plugging the relation (
5.110
) into the equation (
5.53
), we have

(5.113)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,(\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon})-\mu\Delta_{x}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon}+j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})+R_{\varepsilon,u}\,,

where

(5.114)

\displaystyle R_{\varepsilon,u}=\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,R_{\varepsilon,A}-\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,(\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon})\,.

Noticing that
\theta_{\varepsilon}=(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})+\tfrac{2}{5}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})
, we substitute the relation (
5.111
) into the equation (
5.48
) and then obtain

(5.115)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big[\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})\big]-\kappa\Delta_{x}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})=R_{\varepsilon,\theta}\,,

where

(5.116)

\displaystyle R_{\varepsilon,\theta}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{5}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,R_{\varepsilon,B}-\tfrac{2}{5}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big[\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\big]-\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big[\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})\big]

\displaystyle+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{5}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big[\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\big]+\tfrac{2}{5}\kappa\Delta_{x}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\,.

Now we take the limit from (
5.113
) to obtain the
u
-equation of (
1.25
). For any
T>0
, let a vector-valued text function
\psi(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3}))
with
\mathrm{div}_{x}\psi=0
,
\psi(0,x)=\psi_{0}(x)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
and
\psi(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
. We multiply (
5.113
) by
\psi(t,x)
and integrate by parts over
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}
. Then we obtain

(5.117)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\psi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}(0,x)\cdot\psi(0,x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{P}\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{in},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\psi_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\,.

From the initial conditions (3) in Theorem
1.2
and the convergence (
5.71
), we deduce that

(5.118)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{P}\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{in},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\psi_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x\rightarrow\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{P}\langle G^{in},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\psi_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{in}\cdot\psi_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x

and

(5.119)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\rightarrow\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}u\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Namely, we have

(5.120)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\psi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\rightarrow-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{in}\cdot\psi_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}u\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. It is implied by the strong convergences (
5.71
) that

(5.121)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,(\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,(u\otimes u)\quad\textrm{strongly in }\quad C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-2}_{x})\,,

\displaystyle\mu\Delta_{x}\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\mu\Delta_{x}u\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\textrm{strongly in }\quad C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-3}_{x})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(n_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(nE)\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \,\textrm{strongly in }\quad C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})\,,

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
s\geq 3
. Furthermore, we deduce from the convergences (
5.73
) and (
5.71
) that

(5.122)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(j_{\varepsilon}\times B_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(j\times B)

weakly in
L^{2}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

It remains to prove

(5.123)

\displaystyle R_{\varepsilon,u}\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distribution as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
R_{\varepsilon,u}
is defined in (
5.114
). Indeed, by employing the convergences (
5.71
) and (
5.72
), one can obtain

(5.124)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}(\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow 0

weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
and strongly in
H^{s-2}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Moreover, by employing the similar arguments in the convergence (
5.87
), we know that for
\Xi=A
or
B

(5.125)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}\,R_{\varepsilon,\Xi}\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distribution as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
R_{\varepsilon,\Xi}
are defined in (
5.112
). Thus, the convergences (
5.124
) and (
5.125
) imply the convergence (
5.123
). Collecting the limits (
5.120
), (
5.121
), (
5.122
) and (
5.123
) yields that
u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
obeys

(5.126)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+\mathcal{P}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,(u\otimes u)-\mu\Delta_{x}u=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(nE+j\times B)

with the initial data

(5.127)

\displaystyle u(0,x)=\mathcal{P}u^{in}(x)\,.

Finally, we take the limit from (
5.115
) to the third
\theta
-equation in (
1.25
) as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. For any
T>0
, let
\xi(t,x)
be a test function satisfying
\xi(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3}))
with
\xi(0,x)=\xi_{0}(x)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
and
\xi(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
. From the initial conditions (3) in Theorem
1.2
and the strong convergence (
5.71
), we deduce that

(5.128)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{t}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{3}\rho_{\varepsilon})(t,x)\xi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{in},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}\big(\tfrac{3}{5}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)-\tfrac{2}{5}\big)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\xi_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{3}\rho_{\varepsilon})(t,x)\partial_{t}\xi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle G^{in},\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}_{2}\big(\tfrac{3}{5}(\tfrac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)-\tfrac{2}{5}\big)\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\xi_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\theta(t,x)\partial_{t}\xi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\big(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta^{in}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho^{in}\big)\xi_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\theta(t,x)\partial_{t}\xi(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. It is derived from the strong convergence (
5.71
) that

(5.129)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big[\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})\big]\rightarrow\mathrm{div}_{x}\,(u\theta)\quad\textrm{strongly in }\quad C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-2}_{x})\,,

\displaystyle\kappa\Delta_{x}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \,\textrm{strongly in }\qquad C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-3}_{x})

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
s\geq 3
.

It remains to prove

(5.130)

\displaystyle R_{\varepsilon,\theta}\rightarrow 0

in the sense of distribution as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
R_{\varepsilon,\theta}
is defined in (
5.116
). Indeed, from the convergences (
5.71
) and (
5.72
), we derive that

(5.131)

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{5}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big[\mathcal{P}u_{\varepsilon}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\big]+\tfrac{2}{5}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big[\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\big]+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\big[\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u_{\varepsilon}(\tfrac{3}{5}\theta_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho_{\varepsilon})\big]\rightarrow 0

weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
and strongly in
H^{s-2}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Moreover, the convergence (
5.72
) tells us

(5.132)

\displaystyle\kappa\Delta_{x}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow 0

weakly-
\star
in
t\geq 0
and strongly in
H^{s-3}_{x}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
s\geq 3
. Because

(5.133)

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\varepsilon}{5}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon\|j_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\|E_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon

holds by utilizing the uniform bounds (
5.21
) and (
5.32
), we know that

(5.134)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3}j_{\varepsilon}\cdot E_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0

strongly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Consequently, the convergences (
5.125
), (
5.131
), (
5.132
) and (
5.134
) imply the convergence (
5.130
). It is yielded by collecting the convergences (
5.128
), (
5.125
) and (
5.130
) that
\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
subjects to

(5.135)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,(u\theta)=\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta

with the initial data

(5.136)

\displaystyle\theta(0,x)=\tfrac{3}{5}\theta^{in}(x)-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho^{in}(x)\,.

5.3.5.
Equations of the electromagnetic fields
E
and
B

We will derive the equations of
E
and
B
from taking limit in the last five equations of (
5.2
) as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. For any
T>0
, let
\omega(t,x)
be a vector-valued test function such that
\omega(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{T}^{3}))
with
\omega(0,x)=\omega_{0}(x)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
and
\omega(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
. It is deduced from the initial condition (3) in Theorem
1.2
and the strong convergence (
5.71
) that

(5.137)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{t}Y_{\varepsilon}\cdot\omega(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}Y_{\varepsilon}^{in}\cdot\omega_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}Y_{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\omega(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}Y^{in}\cdot\omega_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}Y\cdot\partial_{t}\omega(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where the symbol
Y
can be
E
or
B
. Moreover, from the convergence (
5.71
), one derives that for
Y=E
or
B

(5.138)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\times Y_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\nabla_{x}\times Y\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathrm{div}_{x}\,Y_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\mathrm{div}_{x}\,Y

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-2}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Thus, from the convergences (
5.71
), (
5.73
), (
5.137
) and (
5.138
), the last four equations of (
5.2
) reduce to

(5.139)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}E-\nabla_{x}\times B=-j\,,\\
\partial_{t}B+\nabla_{x}\times E=0\,,\\
\mathrm{div}_{x}\,E=n\,,\quad\mathrm{div}_{x}\,B=0\,,\end{array}\right.

with the initial data

(5.140)

\displaystyle E(0,x)=E^{in}(x)\,,\quad B(0,x)=B^{in}(x)\,.

We now take limit from the local conservation law
\partial_{t}n_{\varepsilon}+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j_{\varepsilon}=0
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. For any
T>0
, we take any scalar test function
\varUpsilon(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3}))
satisfying
\varUpsilon(0,x)=\varUpsilon_{0}(x)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
and
\varUpsilon(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
. Then, from the initial condition (3) in Theorem
1.2
and the strong convergence (
5.71
), we imply that

(5.141)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{t}n_{\varepsilon}\varUpsilon(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{in},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\varUpsilon_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}n_{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}\varUpsilon(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle G^{in},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\varUpsilon_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}n\,\partial_{t}\varUpsilon(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}n^{in}\,\varUpsilon_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}n\,\partial_{t}\varUpsilon(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Moreover, the convergence (
5.73
) implies that

(5.142)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j

weakly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Then, we have derived from the microscopic local conservation law that

(5.143)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}n+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j=0

with the initial data

(5.144)

\displaystyle n(0,x)=n^{in}(x)\,.

5.3.6.
Summarization

Collecting all above convergence results, we have shown that

(u,\theta,n,E,B)\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s-1}_{x})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{s}_{x})

satisfy the following two fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell equations with Ohm’s law

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla_{x}u-\mu\Delta_{x}u+\nabla_{x}p=\tfrac{1}{2}(nE+j\times B)\,,\qquad\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\theta-\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta=0\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \,\rho+\theta=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}E-\nabla_{x}\times B=-j\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \,\mathrm{div}_{x}\,E=n\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}B+\nabla_{x}\times E=0\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathrm{div}_{x}\,B=0\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\qquad\qquad j-nu=\sigma\big(-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n+E+u\times B\big)\,,\qquad\quad\,\ w=\tfrac{3}{2}n\theta\,,\end{array}\right.

with initial data

(5.145)

\displaystyle u(0,x)=\mathcal{P}u^{in}(x)\,,\ \theta(0,x)=\tfrac{3}{5}\theta^{in}(x)-\tfrac{2}{5}\rho^{in}(x)\,,\ E(0,x)=E^{in}(x)\,,\ B(0,x)=B^{in}(x)\,.

Moreover, from the uniform bound (
1.24
) in Theorem
1.1
and the convergence (
5.23
), we have

(5.146)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\big(

\displaystyle\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)(t)\leq\sup_{t\geq 0}\big(\|G_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}+\|E_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})(t)\leq c_{1}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{in},E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})\rightarrow c_{1}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G^{in},E^{in},B^{in})

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Hence

(5.147)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\big(

\displaystyle\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)(t)\leq c_{1}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G^{in},E^{in},B^{in})\leq c_{1}\ell_{0}\,.

Since
G
is of the form (
5.26
) and
\rho+\theta=0
, there are positive generic constants
C_{h}
and
C_{l}
such that

(5.148)

\displaystyle C_{l}\big(\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\theta\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|n\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\leq\|G\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\leq C_{h}\big(\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\theta\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|n\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\,.

Consequently, the solution
(u,\theta,n,E,B)
to the two fluid incompressible Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Maxwell equations (
1.25
) with Ohm’s law constructed above admits the energy bound

(5.149)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\big(

\displaystyle\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\theta\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|n\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|E\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)(t)\leq c_{1}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G^{in},E^{in},B^{in})\leq c_{1}^{*}\ell_{0}\,,

where
c_{1}^{*}=c_{1}+\tfrac{c_{1}}{C_{l}}>0
.

Appendix A
Construction of local solutions

In this section, we will construct a unique local-in-time solution to the perturbed VMB system (
1.9
) for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
, hence prove Proposition
3.1
. Fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, the construction is based on a uniform energy estimate for a sequence of iterating approximate solutions. We consider the following linear iterating approximate sequence
(n\geq 0)
for solving the perturbed VMB system (
1.9
) with initial data (
1.21
):

(A.1)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\big\{\partial_{t}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathscr{L}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\big\}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\cdot v)\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},G_{\varepsilon}^{n})\,,\\
\partial_{t}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}-\nabla_{x}\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\cdot\mathsf{q}v\sqrt{M}\mathrm{d}v\,,\\
\partial_{t}B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+\nabla_{x}\times E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=0\,,\\
\mathrm{div}_{x}\,E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\cdot\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\mathrm{d}v\,,\quad\mathrm{div}_{x}\,B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=0\,,\end{array}\right.

with initial data

(A.2)

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}|_{t=0}=G_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x,v)\,,\ E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}|_{t=0}=E_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x)\,,\ B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}|_{t=0}=B_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x)\,.

We start with
G_{\varepsilon}^{0}(t,x,v)=G_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x,v)
,
E_{\varepsilon}^{0}(t,x)=E_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x)
and
B_{\varepsilon}^{0}(t,x)=B_{\varepsilon}^{in}(x)
. It is standard from the linear theory to verify that the sequence
(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})
is well-defined for all
n\geq 0
on a maximal interval
[0,T_{\varepsilon}^{n})
. Our goal is to get a uniform in
n
estimate for the iterating energy

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{n}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})(t)+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\,.

We notice that
\mathfrak{E}_{n}(0)=\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{in},E_{\varepsilon}^{in},B_{\varepsilon}^{in})
is independent of
n\geq 0
. For simplicity, we denote by
\mathfrak{E}(0)=\mathfrak{E}_{n}(0)
. Furthermore, we call
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t)
is an
instant iterating energy
of
\mathfrak{E}_{n}(t)
, if there is energy functional
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t)
satisfying

(A.4)

\displaystyle c_{*}\mathfrak{E}_{n}(t)\leq\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t)\leq C_{*}\mathfrak{E}_{n}(t)

for some positive constant
c_{*}
and
C_{*}
, which are both independent of
n
and
\varepsilon
. We also denote by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(0)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(0)
for all
n\geq 0
.

Now we derive the following lemma.

Lemma A.1
.

There exist an instant iterating energy
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t)
, small
\ell>0
and
T^{*}>0
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that if
T^{*}\leq\sqrt{\ell}
,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(0)\leq\ell
and
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t)\leq 2\ell
, then

(A.5)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n+1}(t)\leq 2\ell\,.

If Lemma
A.1
holds, we know that
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t)\leq 2\ell
holds for all
n\geq 0
. Namely,
sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathfrak{E}_{n}(t)\leq 2C_{*}\ell
for all
n\geq 0
. In order to complete the proof of Proposition
3.1
, We thereby employ the compactness arguments, take
n\rightarrow\infty
and obtain a solution
(G_{\varepsilon},E_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon})
for any fixed
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
from Lemma
A.1
.

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

For
|m|\leq s\,(s\geq 3)
, taking
\partial^{m}_{x}
derivatives of the first
G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}
-equation in (
A.1
) and taking inner product with
\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}
over
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

(A.6)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\langle\mathscr{L}\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle(\partial^{m}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\cdot v)\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M},\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m}_{x}[(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}],\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle\partial^{m}_{x}\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},G_{\varepsilon}^{n}),\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,.

We derive from the part (3) of Lemma
2.3
that there is a
\lambda>0
such that

(A.7)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\langle\mathscr{L}\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\geq\tfrac{\lambda}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

Next, by the Maxwell system of
(E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})
in (
A.1
), we have

(A.8)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle(\partial^{m}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\cdot v)\mathsf{q}_{1}

\displaystyle\sqrt{M},\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=\big\langle\partial^{m}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\mathsf{q}_{1}v\sqrt{M}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\partial^{m}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}-\nabla_{x}\times\partial^{m}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}-\langle\partial^{m}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\nabla_{x}\times\partial^{m}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\langle\partial^{m}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\|\partial^{m}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial^{m}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\big)\,.

We now estimate the term
\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m}_{x}[(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}],\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
for all
|m|\leq s
. By employing the decomposition
G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}
, we obtain

(A.9)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m}_{x}[(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}],\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{X_{1}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle(\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\mathsf{q}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{X_{2}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle(\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\mathsf{q}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{X_{3}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle(\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\mathsf{q}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{X_{4}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle(\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\mathsf{q}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}\,.

By the Hölder inequality and the Sobolev embedding theory, one easily yields that

(A.10)

\displaystyle X_{1}\leq C\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\leq C\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\,,

and

(A.11)

\displaystyle X_{2}+X_{3}\leq

\displaystyle C\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,

and

(A.12)

\displaystyle X_{4}\leq C\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\leq C\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\,.

In summary, we have

(A.13)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m}_{x}[(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}],\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\Big]\,.

We now employ Lemma
2.4
to estimate the term
\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle\partial^{m}_{x}\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},G_{\varepsilon}^{n}),\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
for all
|m|\leq s
. More precisely,

(A.14)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle\partial^{m}_{x}\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},G_{\varepsilon}^{n}),\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\langle\partial^{m}_{x}\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},G_{\varepsilon}^{n}),\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\big(\|\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\big)\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big(\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\Big)\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\,.

Next we estimate the term
-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
for all
|m|\leq s
. By employing the relation
G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}
, it can be decomposed as four parts:

(A.15)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{Y_{1}}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle\underset{Y_{2}}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle\underset{Y_{3}}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle\underset{Y_{4}}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}\,.

We notice that there is a singular expression

(A.16)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

occurring in the term
Y_{1}
. Recalling the definition of
\mathbb{P}G
in (
1.15
), we have

(A.17)

\displaystyle\nabla_{v}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=(u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+v\theta_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}-\tfrac{1}{2}v\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\,,

where
\mathsf{q}_{2}=[1,1]\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
\theta_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\frac{2}{3}\phi_{6}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
and the vector field
u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
with the components
u^{n+1}_{\varepsilon,i}=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\phi_{2+i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
(
i=1,2,3
). Then we have

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum\limits_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum\limits_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle v\times\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot\big[(\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+v\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\theta_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\mathsf{q}\mathsf{q}_{2}\sqrt{M}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\tfrac{1}{2}v\mathsf{q}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big],\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum\limits_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle(v\times\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}\cdot\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum\limits_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle(v\times\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot v,\big(\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\theta_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big)\cdot\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum\limits_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle(v\times\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\left\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}M\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum\limits_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle(\left\langle v,M\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\times\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}\left\langle G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}

(A.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,.

We thereby know that the term
Y_{1}
does not involve the singularity. Then, the term
Y_{1}
can be bounded by

(A.19)

\displaystyle Y_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\,.

Moreover, the term
Y_{2}
,
Y_{3}
and
Y_{4}
can also be controlled as

(A.20)

\displaystyle Y_{2}+Y_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}})\|\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\,,

and

(A.21)

\displaystyle Y_{4}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}})\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\sum_{|m|\leq s-1}\|\nabla_{v}\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\,.

Consequently, we obtain

(A.22)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\big\langle\mathsf{q}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{x}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{x}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})

\displaystyle\times\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big]\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\,.

Plugging the bounds (
A.7
), (
A.8
), (
A.13
), (
A.14
) and (
A.22
) into the relation (
A.6
) reduces to

(A.23)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)+\tfrac{\lambda}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{s}^{2}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})+C\Big[1+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})

\displaystyle+C\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\Big(\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big)

for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.

Next we estimate the mixed
(x,v)
-derivative energy bound. We first rewrite the
G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}
-equation of (
A.1
) as

(A.24)

\displaystyle\big\{\partial_{t}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathscr{L}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\big\}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}

\displaystyle=-\big\{\partial_{t}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\big\}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\cdot v)\mathsf{q}_{1}\sqrt{M}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},G_{\varepsilon}^{n})\,.

For all
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
and
\alpha\neq 0
, we take the derivative operator
\partial^{m}_{\alpha}
in (
) and take inner product with
\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}
over
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then we obtain

(A.25)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\underset{Z_{1}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathscr{L}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{Z_{2}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\big[(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\sqrt{M}\mathsf{q}_{1}\big],\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}+\underset{Z_{3}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\big[\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big],\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{Z_{4}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\Gamma(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},G_{\varepsilon}^{n}),\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}\ \underset{Z_{5}}{\underbrace{-\left\langle\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle\underset{Z_{6}}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}C_{\alpha}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{v}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\partial^{m}_{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle\underset{Z_{7}}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}C_{\alpha}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\left\langle\mathsf{q}(\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{v}v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m}_{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle\underset{Z_{8}}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{m^{\prime}<m}\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq 1}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}C_{\alpha}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\left\langle\mathsf{q}(\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{v}v\times\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}_{x}B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{m^{\prime}}_{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle\underset{Z_{9}}{\underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\big[v\cdot\nabla_{x}+\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\big]\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}\,.

Recalling the decomposition of
\mathscr{L}
in Lemma
2.3
(1), we have

(A.26)

\displaystyle\big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathscr{L}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=

\displaystyle 2\big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}(\nu(v)\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}),\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle-\big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathscr{K}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,.

From Lemma
2.2
(2), we derive

(A.27)

\displaystyle\big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}(\nu(v)\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}),\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\geq C_{5}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}-C_{6}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,.

Moreover, Lemma
2.3
(2) tells us that for any
\delta>0
, there is a
C(\delta)>0
such that

(A.28)

\displaystyle\big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathscr{K}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq\delta\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+C(\delta)\|\partial^{m}_{x}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,.

Thus taking
\delta=C_{5}>0
,
\lambda_{0}=C_{5}>0
and
\lambda_{1}=C(C_{5})+2C_{6}>0
implies that the quantity
M_{1}
has the lower bound

(A.29)

\displaystyle Z_{1}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\big\langle\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathscr{L}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\geq\tfrac{\lambda_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}-\tfrac{\lambda_{1}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,.

Since
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
and
\alpha\neq 0
,
0\leq|m|\leq s-1
. Then the term
Z_{2}
can be estimated as

(A.30)

\displaystyle Z_{2}\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

by using the Hölder inequality and the Sobolev embedding theory. For the term
Z_{3}
, one can decompose it as

(A.31)

\displaystyle Z_{3}=

\displaystyle\underset{Z_{31}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}C_{\alpha}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\big\langle\partial^{m}_{x}[E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot(\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{v}v\otimes\partial^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{v}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})],\mathsf{q}\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{Z_{32}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle\partial^{m}_{x}[E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot(v\otimes\partial^{\alpha}_{v}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})],\mathsf{q}\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{Z_{33}}{\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle\partial^{m}_{x}[E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot(v\otimes\partial^{\alpha}_{v}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})],\mathsf{q}\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}}

by using
G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}
. Then the terms
Z_{31}
and
Z_{32}
can be bounded by

(A.32)

\displaystyle C\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

and the term
Z_{33}
can be bounded by

(A.33)

\displaystyle C\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

We figure out that the factor

in

can only be estimated by
\nu(v)
, so that it will be pushed to the
\nu
-weight of norms. However, the
v
factor of

will be absorbed by the factor
\sqrt{M}
in
\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}
and the term

does not involve the

(A.34)

\displaystyle Z_{3}\leq C\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})

\displaystyle+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big]\,.

Via Lemma
2.4
and the decomposition
G_{\varepsilon}^{n}=\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}+\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}
, we easily estimate that

(A.35)

\displaystyle Z_{4}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\big(\|\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\big)\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

Next we control the term
Z_{5}
. Recalling the expression of
\mathbb{P}G
in (

), we have

(A.36)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,+}\phi_{1}(v)+\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,-}\phi_{2}(v)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}u_{\varepsilon,i}^{n+1}\phi_{i+2}(v)+\theta_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\phi_{6}(v)\,.

Thus the term

can be estimated by

(A.37)

\displaystyle Z_{5}\leq C\big[\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}\theta_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\big]\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,,

where
|m|\leq s-1
. We further project the first

-equation of (

by multiplying the vectors

,

,

, we deduce that

(A.38)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,+}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big[-\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+\langle\Theta_{n}+\Psi_{n},\phi_{1}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big]\,,\\[4.2679pt]
\partial_{t}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,-}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big[-\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+\langle\Theta_{n}+\Psi_{n},\phi_{2}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big]\,,\\[4.2679pt]
\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big[-\partial_{i}(\tfrac{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,+}+\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,-}}{2}+\theta_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})+\langle\Theta_{n}+\Psi_{n},\phi_{i+2}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big]\quad(1\leq i\leq 3)\,,\\[4.2679pt]
\partial_{t}\theta_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\big[-\tfrac{2}{3}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+\tfrac{1}{3}\langle\Theta_{n}+\Psi_{n},\phi_{6}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big]\,,\end{array}\right.

where

(A.39)

\displaystyle\Theta_{n}=

\displaystyle-\Big(v\cdot\nabla_{x}+\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}-\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\Big)\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\,,

\displaystyle\Psi_{n}=

\displaystyle-\mathsf{q}(\varepsilon E_{\varepsilon}^{n}+v\times B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}+\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon\mathsf{q}(E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\cdot v)\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\,.

For the quantity

, we derive from the first equation of (

) that for all

(A.40)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\partial^{m}_{x}\langle\Theta_{n},\phi_{1}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\partial^{m}_{x}\langle\Psi_{n},\phi_{1}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\,.

Combining the definitions of

and

in (

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\big(1+\varepsilon\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\,,

and the quantity
\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\partial^{m}_{x}\langle\Psi_{n},\phi_{1}(v)\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}
can be bounded by

(A.42)

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\big(\varepsilon\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\,.

Therefore, we have

(A.43)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\big(1+\varepsilon\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\Big[1+\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\,.

Furthermore, by the analogous arguments in estimating the norm
\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}
in (
A.43
), we easily yield that

(A.44)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{n+1,-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}\partial^{m}_{x}\theta_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\Big[1+\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\,.

Collecting the estimates (
A.37
), (
A.43
) and (
A.44
) gives

(A.45)

\displaystyle Z_{5}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\Big[1+\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

It is easy to know that the term
Z_{6}
is bounded by

(A.46)

\displaystyle Z_{6}\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

and
Z_{7}
is estimated by

(A.47)

\displaystyle Z_{7}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s}_{x,v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

Finally, one notices that the terms
Z_{8}
and
Z_{9}
will be dominated by

(A.48)

\displaystyle Z_{8}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,,

and

(A.49)

\displaystyle Z_{9}\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\big(1+\varepsilon\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\big)\|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{P}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon}\Big[1+\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\,.

Consequently, by substituting the bounds (
A.29
), (
A.30
), (
A.34
), (
A.35
), (
A.45
), (
A.46
), (
A.47
), (
A.48
), (
A.49
) into the equality (
A.25
) and employing the Young’s inequality, we obtain

(A.50)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{\lambda_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{\lambda_{*}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|<|\alpha|}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+C\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})+C\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})

\displaystyle+C\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{\widetilde{H}^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big]

for all
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
\alpha\neq 0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.

We now claim that for any given
1\leq k\leq s
and
|\alpha|\leq s
, there are positive constants
a_{|\alpha|}
,
b_{k}
,
r_{k}
and
C_{k}^{\prime}
, independent of
\varepsilon
and
n
, such that

(A.51)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\\
1\leq|\alpha|\leq k\end{subarray}}a_{|\alpha|}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{b_{k}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\tfrac{r_{k}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\\
1\leq|\alpha|\leq k\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}^{\prime}\bigg\{\Big[1+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\Big]

\displaystyle+\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\Big(\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big)\bigg\}

for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.

We will verify the bound (
A.51
) by induction. If
k=1
,
|\alpha^{\prime}|<|\alpha|=k
implies
\alpha^{\prime}=0
. Then there is a constant
\lambda_{*}^{\prime}>0
, independent of
\varepsilon
and
n
, such that

(A.52)

\displaystyle\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\,,|\alpha|=1}\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|<|\alpha|}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\leq\lambda_{*}^{\prime}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\,.

Then, summing up for
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
|\alpha|=1
in (
A.50
), multiplying it by
\tfrac{\lambda}{2\lambda_{*}\lambda_{*}^{\prime}}
and adding it to the inequality (
A.23
) imply that the inequality (
A.51
) holds for the case
k=1
, where
a_{1}=\tfrac{\lambda}{2\lambda_{*}\lambda_{*}^{\prime}}>0
,
b_{1}=\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda>0
,
r_{1}=\tfrac{\lambda_{0}\lambda}{2\lambda_{*}\lambda_{*}^{\prime}}>0
and
C_{1}^{\prime}>0
are determined by the coefficients in left-hand of (
A.23
) and (
A.50
). Now we assume that the inequality (
A.51
) is valid for
k
. For
|\alpha|=k+1
, summing up for
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
|\alpha|=k+1
in the inequality (
A.50
), we obtain

(A.53)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\,,|\alpha|=k+1}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\tfrac{\lambda_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\,,|\alpha|=k+1}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{N_{k+1}\lambda_{*}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\,,|\alpha|\leq k}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+CN_{k+1}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})

\displaystyle+\Big[1+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big(\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big)\bigg\}\,.

Here
N_{k+1}>0
denotes the number of all possible
(m,\alpha)
such that
|m|+|\alpha|\leq s
with
|\alpha|=k+1
. By the assumption of induction, (
A.51
) holds for the case
k
. In order to absorb the first term on the right-hand side of (
A.53
) by the last term on the left-hand side of (
A.51
), we multiply (
A.53
) by
\tfrac{r_{k}}{2\lambda_{*}N_{k+1}}
and add it to (
A.51
). We then have

(A.54)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})+\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\,,1\leq|\alpha|\leq k+1}a_{|\alpha|}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{b_{k}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\tfrac{r_{k}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\,,1\leq|\alpha|\leq k+1}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k+1}^{\prime}\bigg\{\Big[1+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},E_{\varepsilon}^{n+1},B_{\varepsilon}^{n+1})\Big]

\displaystyle+\Big[\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})+\mathbb{E}_{s}(G_{\varepsilon}^{n},E_{\varepsilon}^{n},B_{\varepsilon}^{n})\Big]\Big(\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big)\bigg\}\,,

where
a_{k+1}=\tfrac{r_{k}}{2N_{k+1}}>0
,
b_{k+1}=b_{k}>0
,
r_{k+1}=\min\big\{\tfrac{r_{k}}{2},\tfrac{\lambda_{0}r_{k}}{2\lambda_{*}N_{k+1}}\big\}>0
and
C_{k+1}^{\prime}=C_{k}^{\prime}+\tfrac{Cr_{k}}{2\lambda_{*}}>0
. Then the induction principle implies that the bound (
A.51
) holds.

We now introduce a so-called instant iterating energy of
\mathfrak{E}_{n}(t)

(A.55)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t)=

\displaystyle\|G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|E_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\\
1\leq|\alpha|\leq k\end{subarray}}a_{|\alpha|}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\Big[b_{s}\|\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+r_{s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m|+|\alpha|\leq s\\
1\leq|\alpha|\leq k\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m}_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}^{\perp}G_{\varepsilon}^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\Big](\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\,.

It is easy to verify that

(A.56)

\displaystyle c_{*}\mathfrak{E}_{n}(t)\leq\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t)\leq C_{*}\mathfrak{E}_{n}(t)\,,

where
c_{*}=\min\{1,a_{1},\cdots,a_{s},b_{s},r_{s}\}>0
and
C_{*}=\max\{1,a_{1},\cdots,a_{s},b_{s},r_{s}\}>0
. We take
k=s
in the inequality (
A.51
) and integrate over
[0,t]
. Then we derive from the fact
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t)\leq 2\ell
that

(A.57)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n+1}(t)\leq\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n+1}(0)+C_{s}(1+\ell)\Big[\ell t+t\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n+1}(s)+\sqrt{\ell}\ \widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n+1}(t)\Big]\,,

where
C_{s}=\tfrac{12C_{s}^{\prime}}{c_{*}}(1+\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{c_{*}}}+\tfrac{1}{c_{*}})>0
. Here
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n+1}(0)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(0)\leq\ell
. It follows that for
t\leq T^{*}

(A.58)

\displaystyle\Big[1-C_{s}(1+\ell)T^{*}-C_{s}(1+\ell)\sqrt{\ell}\Big]\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n+1}(t)\leq\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n+1}(0)+C_{s}(1+\ell)\ell T^{*}

\displaystyle\leq\ell+C_{s}(1+\ell)\ell T^{*}\,.

If
0<T^{*}\leq\sqrt{\ell}
and
\ell>0
is small such that
(1+\ell)\sqrt{\ell}\leq\tfrac{1}{5C_{s}}
, then

(A.59)

\displaystyle 1-C_{s}(1+\ell)T^{*}-C_{s}(1+\ell)\sqrt{\ell}\geq 1-2C_{s}(1+\ell)\sqrt{\ell}\,.

Consequently, we have

(A.60)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{n+1}(t)\leq\Big[1+\tfrac{3C_{s}(1+\ell)\sqrt{\ell}}{1-2C_{s}(1+\ell)\sqrt{\ell}}\Big]\ell\leq 2\ell\,.

The proof of Lemma
A.1
is finished.
∎
